### Appendix E: Quick reference card
```
`# List recipes
python3 -m poler_toolkit list

# Quick structural check (fastest)
python3 -m poler_toolkit structure FILE --label my_run

# Deep dive on a keyword
python3 -m poler_toolkit analyze FILE --keyword NAME --label my_run

# Compare two files
python3 -m poler_toolkit compare FILE1 FILE2 --label cmp

# Character map (explicit names recommended)
python3 -m poler_toolkit characters FILE --names "A,B,C"

# Extension recipes — direct module invocation
python3 -m poler_toolkit.recipes_ext.theme_evolution FILE
python3 -m poler_toolkit.recipes_ext.themes_alpha FILE
python3 -m poler_toolkit.recipes_ext.diff_versions FILE1 FILE2
python3 -m poler_toolkit.recipes_ext.batch_directory /path/to/dir
python3 -m poler_toolkit.recipes_ext.custom_plugin PLUGIN.py TARGET...

# HTTP API
python3 -m poler_toolkit.serve --port 8000
curl http://127.0.0.1:8000/recipes
curl -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8000/structure \
     -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
     -d '{"filepaths":["FILE"]}'
curl http://127.0.0.1:8000/tasks/<task_id>`
```
```
`# Python — via runner (4 core recipes only)
from poler_toolkit import runner
out_dir = runner.run("structure", ["/path/to/file"], task_label="my_run")

# Python — direct recipe call (all 9 recipes, full option set)
from poler_toolkit.recipes_ext import recipe_theme_evolution
bundle = recipe_theme_evolution(["/path/to/file"], top_n=15, out_dir=out_dir)

# Python — read the result
import json
report = json.loads((out_dir / "report.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
print(report["verdict"])
print(report["metrics"])`
```

## Example 1
### Compare two versions of a novel (`diff_versions`)
> **Scenario**: An author sends you `chapter_v1.txt` and `chapter_v2.txt`. You want a structured diff: which chapters are the same / changed / removed / added, and how much each changed chapter drifted.
**Setup** — for this example we use two close-but-different files: the Cassiopeia canon (`.txt`) and the Сфера Предела EPUB. In real life you'd diff two drafts of the same novel; here we use two unrelated files to exercise the "added/removed/changed" classifier on every chapter.
**Input**:
```
`$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE   (107 652 words, 35 chapters)
$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE_2                        (172 865 words, 45 chapters)`
```
**Command** (CLI — note `diff_versions` isn't wired into `cli.py` yet, so use the module directly):
```
`cd /path/to/poler_toolkit
python3 -m poler_toolkit.recipes_ext.diff_versions \
    "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE" \
    "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE_2"`
```
**Python equivalent**:
```
`from pathlib import Path
from poler_toolkit.recipes_ext import recipe_diff_versions
from poler_toolkit import report

out_dir = Path("$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/diff_cas_vs_sphere")
out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

bundle = recipe_diff_versions(
    [
        "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE",
        "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE_2",
    ],
    keyword=None,        # auto-detect across both files
    window_size=3000,
    lang="auto",
    out_dir=out_dir,
)
report.save_json_report(bundle["report"], out_dir, "report.json")
report.save_markdown_verdict(bundle["verdict_lines"], out_dir, "verdict.md")`
```
**Output** — 4 PNG + `diff.md` + `report.json` + `verdict.md` in `$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/diff_versions_<timestamp>/`:
```
`01_diff_heatmap.png          v1 × v2 chapter cosine similarity matrix
02_changes_per_chapter.png   Added / removed / changed tokens per chapter
03_wordcount_comparison.png  Side-by-side word count per chapter
04_semantic_drift.png        Best-match cosine per v1 chapter
diff.md                      Raw POLER format_diff_markdown output
report.json
verdict.md`
```
**Key `report.json` metrics** (real run):
```
`{
  "meta": {
    "task": "diff_versions",
    "engine": "POLER v6 diff_files + chapter TF-IDF cosine + difflib",
    "timestamp": "2026-06-29T…",
    "toolkit_version": "1.0.0"
  },
  "inputs": [
    "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE",
    "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE_2"
  ],
  "metrics": {
    "poler_diff": {
      "file1_epsilon":   7.34,
      "file2_epsilon":   9.12,
      "delta_epsilon":   1.78,
      "file1_windows":   222,
      "file2_windows":   358,
      "delta_windows":   136
    },
    "chapter_classification": [
      {"side": "v1", "chapter_index": 0,  "title": "Пролог: Останній Місяць",
       "status": "changed", "best_match_index": 12,
       "best_match_similarity": 0.41, "tokens_added": 412, "tokens_removed": 388},
      {"side": "v1", "chapter_index": 1,  "title": "Глава 3: Тріщина, Що Дихае",
       "status": "removed", "best_match_index": 7,
       "best_match_similarity": 0.18, "tokens_added": 0, "tokens_removed": 0},
      {"side": "v2", "chapter_index": 0,  "title": "Глава 1",
       "status": "added", "best_match_index": 3,
       "best_match_similarity": 0.09, "tokens_added": 0, "tokens_removed": 0},
      // … one entry per chapter across both files
    ],
    "same_threshold":   0.95,
    "match_threshold":  0.30,
    "n_v1_chapters":    35,
    "n_v2_chapters":    45,
    "n_same":           0,
    "n_changed":        21,
    "n_removed":        14,
    "n_added":          24
  },
  "outputs": {
    "diff_heatmap":            "<output_root>/01_diff_heatmap.png",
    "changes_per_chapter":     "<output_root>/02_changes_per_chapter.png",
    "wordcount_comparison":    "<output_root>/03_wordcount_comparison.png",
    "semantic_drift":          "<output_root>/04_semantic_drift.png",
    "diff_md":                 "<output_root>/diff.md"
  },
  "verdict": "v1 vs v2: 21 chapters changed (avg drift 0.38), 14 removed, 24 added. POLER Δε = +1.78 (v2 has higher keyword energy)."
}`
```
**Interpretation**:
- **`poler_diff.delta_epsilon = +1.78`** — the POLER "energy" grew from v1 to v2, meaning v2 has more semantic concentration around the auto-detected keyword. Read this together with `delta_windows = +136` (v2 has 60 % more analysis windows → it's longer, so a higher ε is expected).
- **`n_changed = 21, n_removed = 14, n_added = 24`** — most chapters don't have a clean 1:1 match; this is expected because the two files are unrelated novels. For a real version-diff of the same novel, expect mostly `changed` and a handful of `added`/`removed`.
- **`best_match_similarity` range**: this is what the "drift curve" PNG plots. Spikes at 1.0 = identical chapters; dips near 0 = chapters that were rewritten beyond recognition.
- **`same_threshold = 0.95`**: chapters with cosine ≥ 0.95 are flagged `same`. Lower this if your text uses heavy punctuation changes that depress cosine.
> **Pitfall** (see [AI_USAGE.md → Common Pitfalls #3]): the Сфера Предела EPUB has only 51 paragraphs across 45 chapters. This skews the chapter classification toward `added`/`removed` because per-chapter TF-IDF vectors become unreliable. For a fair comparison, convert the EPUB to `.txt` first or pre-process to inject paragraph breaks.
---
## Example 2
### Find outliers in a directory (`batch_directory`)
> **Scenario**: You have 20 short stories in a folder and want to find which one is the odd one out — different genre, different author, or just structurally anomalous.
**Setup** — for this example we'll point at the `notebooklm_studio/` subfolder of `./upload/`, which contains 9 Markdown essays on Etheria canon.
**Input**: a directory path.
**Command**:
```
`cd /path/to/poler_toolkit
python3 -m poler_toolkit.recipes_ext.batch_directory \
    ./notebooklm_studio \
    --extensions .md`
```
**Python equivalent**:
```
`from pathlib import Path
from poler_toolkit.recipes_ext import recipe_batch_directory
from poler_toolkit import report

out_dir = Path("$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/batch_notebooklm")
out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

bundle = recipe_batch_directory(
    ["./notebooklm_studio"],
    extensions=[".md"],
    recursive=False,
    top_n=10,
    out_dir=out_dir,
)
report.save_json_report(bundle["report"], out_dir, "report.json")
report.save_markdown_verdict(bundle["verdict_lines"], out_dir, "verdict.md")`
```
**Output** — 5 PNG + 3 JSON/MD files:
```
`01_file_similarity.png     9×9 TF-IDF cosine heatmap
02_pca_files.png           2-D PCA, each file labelled
03_wordcounts.png          Word counts per file (sorted desc)
04_entropy_dist.png        Histogram of Shannon entropy values
05_outliers_radar.png      Radar chart of normalised metrics for top-3 outliers
per_file_report.json       Full per-file metrics
poler_directory.md         POLER format_directory_markdown output
report.json
verdict.md`
```
**Key `report.json` metrics** (illustrative — actual numbers depend on the directory contents):
```
`{
  "meta": { "task": "batch_directory", "engine": "POLER v6 analyze_directory + sklearn", ... },
  "inputs": ["./notebooklm_studio"],
  "metrics": {
    "n_files": 9,
    "extensions_used": [".md"],
    "recursive": false,
    "per_file": [
      {"path": "NotebookLM_Физика эфирного боя…md",  "words": 4123, "chars": 31245,
       "lines": 178, "entropy": 9.12, "avg_word_len": 5.7,
       "top5_words": [["этерия", 89], ["сфера", 67], ["рейд", 41], ...]},
      // … 9 entries
    ],
    "similarity_matrix": [[/* 9×9 cosine */]],
    "pca_coords": [[0.12, -0.34], [0.45, 0.21], ...],  // 9 files
    "outliers": [
      {"path": "NotebookLM_Двойная слепота Экхарта…md",
       "max_similarity": 0.18,
       "reason": "max cosine to any other file = 0.18 (< 0.20 threshold)"},
      // … up to 3 outliers
    ],
    "poler_directory": { /* raw poler_v6.analyze_directory result */ }
  },
  "outputs": { ... },
  "verdict": "Analyzed 9 files. Top outliers: 'NotebookLM_Двойная слепота Экхарта…' (max_sim=0.18), ..."
}`
```
**Interpretation**:
- **`outliers`** is the headline result — each entry has `max_similarity` (the highest cosine to any other file). Below `OUTLIER_THRESHOLD = 0.20` means the file has no close neighbour in the corpus.
- **`pca_coords`** gives you the 2-D position of each file. Plot them yourself if you want a custom scatter.
- **`per_file[i].entropy`** reveals vocabulary richness per file. A file with entropy < 8.0 is probably very repetitive (a list, a glossary, a TOC).
- **`poler_directory`** wraps `poler_v6.analyze_directory(...)` — POLER's own cross-file clusters and per-file ε values. Cross-reference with the sklearn PCA to find files that POLER groups together but sklearn separates (often means stylistic similarity but topical difference).
> **Pitfall**: very short files (< 200 words) produce noisy TF-IDF vectors and dominate the outlier list. Filter them out before running.

|   глав с маркером | 9/35 |

|   density | 7.82/1000 |
| Глава минимум плотности | #4 «Глава 5: Безіменне небо (Фінальна версія)» |
|   density | 0.00/1000 |

## Вердикт

«хаос» — самый волатильный (var=0.5220, mean=0.28/1000, в 9/35 главах).

**Real per-chapter emotional density** (first 10 of 35 chapters, markers/1000 tokens):
```
`Chapter 1 (Пролог):                          2.080   ← high emotional density
Chapter 2 (Глава 3: Тріщина):                0.884
Chapter 3 (Глава 4: Рождение Обсидиана):     1.442
Chapter 4 (Глава 5: Безіменне небо):         0.000   ← no markers fire at all
Chapter 5 (Глава 6: Нитка):                  0.269
Chapter 6 (Глава 7):                         2.022   ← another peak
Chapter 7 (Глава 8):                         0.318
Chapter 8 (Глава 9):                         0.000
Chapter 9 (Глава 10):                        1.207
Chapter 10 (Глава 11):                       2.454
...
Chapter 33 (Глава 59: Угода з Дияволом):     7.820   ← global peak`
```
**Interpretation**:
- **19 of 60 markers fire** — a curated emotional vocabulary is in use, but not the full set. The 41 unused markers (e.g. `свобода`, `надія`) indicate themes the text doesn't explore.
- **`хаос` (chaos) is the most volatile** — it spikes in specific chapters (battle scenes, confrontation chapters). Mean density 0.282/1000 = roughly 1 occurrence per 3 500 tokens.
- **`реальність` (reality) is the most stable** — appears at a steady rate of 0.06/1000 in 8/35 chapters. This is the thematic undercurrent.
- **Chapter 33 (`Глава 59: Угода з Дияволом`)** has 7.82 markers/1000 — 27× the average. This is the emotional climax of the novel.
- **Chapters 4 and 8** have 0.00 markers/1000 — they're either purely expository or use vocabulary outside POLER's curated marker set.
> **Tip**: stack `analyze` (for the keyword-specific POLER data) with `theme_evolution` (for the marker timeline) to get the full emotional picture. The `02_chapter_matrix_poler.png` artifact from `analyze` and the `02_heatmap.png` artifact from `theme_evolution` show related information at different granularities.

## Example 4
### Build a custom analyzer plugin (`custom_plugin`)
> **Scenario**: You want to compute the **type-token ratio (TTR)** of a file — a metric the toolkit doesn't ship. Write a 30-line Python plugin, run it through `custom_plugin`, get the standard JSON+MD+PNG wrapper.
**Input plugin** (`/tmp/ttr_plugin.py`):
```
`"""TTR (type-token ratio) plugin for poler_toolkit."""
from pathlib import Path
from collections import Counter

DEFAULT_MIN_WORD_LEN = 2

def run(filepaths, opts, poler, toolkit_core, toolkit_viz, out_dir):
    min_len = opts.get("min_word_len", DEFAULT_MIN_WORD_LEN)
    results = {}
    total_tokens = 0
    total_unique = 0

    for fp in filepaths:
        text = Path(fp).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        tokens = [w.lower() for w in text.split() if len(w) >= min_len]
        unique = len(set(tokens))
        ttr = unique / max(len(tokens), 1)
        results[Path(fp).name] = {
            "tokens": len(tokens),
            "unique": unique,
            "ttr": round(ttr, 4),
        }
        total_tokens += len(tokens)
        total_unique += unique

    overall_ttr = total_unique / max(total_tokens, 1)
    return {
        "metrics": {
            "per_file":   results,
            "total_tokens": total_tokens,
            "total_unique": total_unique,
            "overall_ttr":  round(overall_ttr, 4),
            "min_word_len": min_len,
        },
        "verdict": f"Overall TTR = {overall_ttr:.3f} ({total_unique} unique / {total_tokens} tokens)",
        "artifacts": {},
        "extra_lines": [
            "",
            "## Per-file TTR",
            "",
            "| File | Tokens | Unique | TTR |",
            "|---|---|---|---|",
            *[f"| {name} | {d['tokens']} | {d['unique']} | {d['ttr']} |"
              for name, d in results.items()],
        ],
    }`
```
**Command**:
```
`cd /path/to/poler_toolkit
python3 -m poler_toolkit.recipes_ext.custom_plugin \
    /tmp/ttr_plugin.py \
    "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE" \
    "./sfera_predela.txt"`
```
**Python equivalent**:
```
`from pathlib import Path
from poler_toolkit.recipes_ext import recipe_custom_plugin
from poler_toolkit import report

out_dir = Path("$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/ttr_plugin_run")
out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

bundle = recipe_custom_plugin(
    [
        "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE",
        "./sfera_predela.txt",
    ],
    plugin_path="/tmp/ttr_plugin.py",
    timeout=60,
    plugin_opts={"min_word_len": 3},
    out_dir=out_dir,
)
report.save_json_report(bundle["report"], out_dir, "report.json")
report.save_markdown_verdict(bundle["verdict_lines"], out_dir, "verdict.md")`
```
**Expected `report.json`** (illustrative — actual TTR depends on the texts):
```
`{
  "meta": {
    "task": "custom_plugin",
    "engine": "custom plugin (ttr_plugin.py) + poler_toolkit wrapper",
    "timestamp": "2026-06-29T…",
    "toolkit_version": "1.0.0"
  },
  "inputs": [
    "/tmp/ttr_plugin.py",
    "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE",
    "./sfera_predela.txt"
  ],
  "metrics": {
    "plugin_metrics": {
      "per_file": {
        "Касіопея…txt": {"tokens": 95000, "unique": 12300, "ttr": 0.1295},
        "sfera_predela.txt": {"tokens": 165000, "unique": 18900, "ttr": 0.1145}
      },
      "total_tokens": 260000,
      "total_unique": 31200,
      "overall_ttr": 0.1200,
      "min_word_len": 3
    },
    "plugin_path": "/tmp/ttr_plugin.py",
    "n_targets": 2,
    "status": "ok",
    "execution_time_seconds": 0.42,
    "tracemalloc_peak_kib": 21000.0,
    "rss_delta_kib": 18000,
    "sandbox_events": [],
    "sandbox_events_count": 0
  },
  "outputs": {
    "plugin_info": "<output_root>/plugin_info.md"
  },
  "verdict": "Overall TTR = 0.120 (31200 unique / 260000 tokens)"
}`
```
**Real `verdict.md` from a similar run** (using Agent E's test_plugin.py on Cassiopeia only):
```
`# Custom plugin: test_plugin.py

| Поле | Значение |
|---|---|
| Plugin path | $POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_PLUGIN |
| Targets | 1 |
| Timeout | 300s |
| Execution time | 0.420s |
| tracemalloc peak | 10,671.7 KiB |
| RSS delta | 21,480 KiB |
| Sandbox events | 1 |
| Status | OK |

## Sandbox access log (allowed, logged)

| Category | Count |
|----------|-------|
| os.environ | 1 |

### First 10 events

| # | Category | Detail |
|---|----------|--------|
| 1 | os.environ | `get('MPLBACKEND')` |

## Plugin extra output

## Test plugin output

This is a test.

- Plugin: **word_count_test** v1.0.0
- Files processed: 1
- Total words: 107652
- MPLBACKEND env: `(not set)`


## Вердикт

**Processed 1 files, 107652 words total**`
```
**Interpretation**:
- The plugin's `metrics` dict is wrapped under `metrics.plugin_metrics` so it doesn't collide with toolkit-internal fields (`plugin_path`, `status`, `execution_time_seconds`, `tracemalloc_peak_kib`, `sandbox_events`).
- **`status: "ok"`** vs `"recipe_error"` — the latter means the plugin raised an exception or returned a malformed dict; check `error` and `traceback` fields.
- **`tracemalloc_peak_kib`** vs **`rss_delta_kib`** — the first is Python-allocated memory, the second is process RSS delta. RSS > tracemalloc means the plugin loaded C extensions or used numpy.
- **`sandbox_events`** lists every `os.environ` / `subprocess` / `socket` access. The test_plugin accessed `os.environ.get('MPLBACKEND')` (because matplotlib checks it on import) — this is benign, but a plugin that spawns subprocesses or opens sockets deserves scrutiny.
> **Tip**: keep `timeout` low (60–120 s) for simple plugins. The recipe daemonises the worker thread on timeout, so a runaway plugin can leak resources. Always test your plugin standalone first (`python3 /tmp/ttr_plugin.py`) before wrapping it in `custom_plugin`.

## Example 5
### Quick health check on an EPUB (`structure`)
> **Scenario**: Someone hands you an EPUB and you want a 15-second structural diagnosis — is it well-paragraphed, does it cluster naturally, what's the entropy?
**Input**: `$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE_2`
**Command**:
```
`cd /path/to/poler_toolkit
python3 -m poler_toolkit structure \
    "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE_2" \
    --label doc_test_structure_sphere`
```
**Real `report.json`**:
```
`{
  "meta": {
    "task": "structure",
    "engine": "sklearn (TF-IDF + KMeans + PCA)",
    "timestamp": "2026-06-29T12:44:58",
    "toolkit_version": "1.0.0"
  },
  "inputs": ["$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE_2"],
  "metrics": {
    "total_words": 172865,
    "total_chapters": 45,
    "total_paragraphs": 51,
    "neighbor_cosine_mean": 0.5605808792236016,
    "neighbor_cosine_std":  0.11553794479827806,
    "frac_below_0.10": 0.02,
    "frac_above_0.20": 0.98,
    "best_k": 3,
    "silhouette": 0.06954342631518613,
    "pca_explained_variance": 0.15115320577329217,
    "chap_sim_mean_offdiag":  0.4775858980706261,
    "entropy_mean": 9.438567354192411,
    "entropy_std":  0.5762981602977391
  },
  "outputs": {
    "chapter_matrix": "<output_root>/01_chapter_matrix.png",
    "transition":     "<output_root>/02_transition.png",
    "pca":            "<output_root>/03_pca.png",
    "entropy":        "<output_root>/04_entropy.png"
  },
  "verdict": "ЗМIШАНА КАРТИНА — окремi блоки тримаються, iншi розсипаються."
}`
```
**Real `verdict.md`**:
```
`# Структура: Сфера Предела .epub

| Метрика | Значение |
|---|---|
| Слов | 172865 |
| Глав | 45 |
| Абзацев | 51 |
| Сосед. cosine | 0.561 |
| Доля <0.10 | 2.0% |
| Доля >0.20 | 98.0% |
| Silhouette | 0.070 |
| PCA expl.var | 0.151 |
| Cosine глав | 0.478 |
| Entropy | 9.44 |

## Вердикт

**ЗМIШАНА КАРТИНА — окремi блоки тримаються, iншi розсипаються.**`
```
**Interpretation**:
- **`total_paragraphs = 51` for 45 chapters and 172 865 words** — that's only 1.13 paragraphs per chapter and 3 389 words per paragraph. **Red flag**: the EPUB lost its paragraph breaks during extraction. A well-formatted novel of this size should have ~2 000 paragraphs.
- **`neighbor_cosine_mean = 0.561`** is **suspiciously high** for prose (normal: 0.10–0.40). This is a symptom of the paragraph-break issue: most "adjacent paragraph" pairs are actually within the same mega-paragraph, so they share vocabulary.
- **`frac_above_0.20 = 98.0%`** confirms the diagnosis — virtually every paragraph pair is highly similar.
- **`silhouette = 0.070`** is low → no natural clusters. With 51 fake-paragraphs, KMeans finds 3 groups but they're not meaningful.
- **`pca_explained_variance = 0.151`** is decent (15 % of variance in 2-D), so the PCA scatter plot is somewhat interpretable.
- **`entropy = 9.44`** is normal-high for Russian prose — the vocabulary is rich.
**Action**:
- The structural metrics are unreliable for this EPUB. Convert it to `.txt` first:
```
`   ebook-convert "Сфера Предела .epub" "Сфера Предела.txt"
   # or extract manually:
   python3 -c "import poler_v6; print(poler_v6.read_epub('Сфера Предела .epub'))" > sphere.txt`
```
- Or use `--lang inline_ru` so chapters are detected by `ГЛАВА N` anywhere in the text (works around missing line-start anchors).

## Example 6
### Compare two unrelated novels (`compare`)
> **Scenario**: You want to know whether author A (Cassiopeia) or author B (Сфера Предела) writes more coherently, has richer vocabulary, etc.
**Input**: two files.
**Command**:
```
`cd /path/to/poler_toolkit
python3 -m poler_toolkit compare \
    "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE" \
    "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE_2" \
    --label doc_test_compare`
```
**Real `verdict.md`**:
```
`# Сравнение: Касіопея исп канон главы 1 -58 (1).txt vs Сфера Предела .epub

| Метрика | Касіопея исп канон г | Сфера Предела  | Ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| Сосед. cosine | 0.071 | 0.561 | 7.89× |
| Silhouette | 0.006 | 0.070 | 12.30× |
| Cosine глав | 0.582 | 0.478 | 0.82× |
| PCA expl.var | 0.014 | 0.151 | 10.77× |
| Доля скачков <0.10 | 77.4% | 2.0% | 38.71× |
| Слов | 107652 | 172865 |  |
| Глав | 35 | 45 |  |
| POLER clusters | 7 | 8 |  |
| Entropy mean | 8.88 | 9.44 |  |

## Вердикт

**Сфера Предела  vs Касіопея исп канон главы 1 -58 (1): связность соседних абзацев в 7.9× (0.561 vs 0.071);
silhouette в 12.3× (0.070 vs 0.006); PCA expl.var в 10.8× (0.151 vs 0.014);
резких скачков в 38.7× меньше.**`
```
**Real `ratios` from `report.json`**:
```
`{
  "ratios": {
    "coherence_ratio":     7.89401035506394,
    "silhouette_ratio":   12.29725075124599,
    "chap_sim_ratio":      0.8202953620072949,
    "pca_variance_ratio": 10.7660956474916,
    "frac_sharp_ratio":   38.70853916725476
  }
}`
```
**Interpretation**:
- **Coherence ratio 7.89×** — Сфера Предела has 7.9× higher paragraph-to-paragraph cosine. **But** (see Example 5) this is partly an artefact of the EPUB's missing paragraph breaks. With broken paragraph detection, every "paragraph" is a multi-page chunk, so adjacent chunks naturally share vocabulary.
- **Silhouette ratio 12.3×** — same caveat. The 51 fake-paragraphs of Сфера cluster more cleanly than the 2 835 real paragraphs of Cassiopeia because there are fewer of them.
- **`chap_sim_ratio = 0.82×`** — Cassiopeia's chapters are slightly more lexically similar to each other than Сфера's, suggesting more cross-chapter vocabulary overlap (recurring characters/settings).
- **`frac_sharp_ratio = 38.71×`** — Cassiopeia has 38× more sharp paragraph transitions. With proper paragraphing on both sides, this would be a real stylistic difference; here it's mostly an EPUB artefact.
**Lesson**: always sanity-check `compare` results against `structure` runs on each file individually. A ratio > 5× is almost always a sign that one of the files has a formatting issue, not a real stylistic gap.
---
## Example 7
### HTTP API end-to-end
> **Scenario**: Submit a recipe via `curl`, poll for completion, and read the result — all from the shell.
**Start the server** in one terminal:
```
`cd /path/to/poler_toolkit
python3 -m poler_toolkit.serve --port 8000 --verbose`
```
**Step 1 — check the service**:
```
`curl -s http://127.0.0.1:8000/ | python3 -m json.tool`
```
Real response:
```
`{
  "name": "POLER Toolkit HTTP API",
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "time": "2026-06-29T12:55:00",
  "recipes": [
    "analyze", "compare", "structure", "characters",
    "theme_evolution", "themes_alpha", "diff_versions",
    "batch_directory", "custom_plugin"
  ]
}`
```
**Step 2 — submit a `structure` job**:
```
`curl -s -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8000/structure \
     -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
     -d '{
           "filepaths": ["$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE"],
           "options": {"lang": "auto"}
         }' | python3 -m json.tool`
```
Real response:
```
`{
  "status": "pending",
  "task_id": "63ebc319f926",
  "recipe": "structure",
  "submitted_at": "2026-06-29T12:35:27",
  "poll_url": "/tasks/63ebc319f926"
}`
```
**Step 3 — poll until done**:
```
`# Poll once
curl -s http://127.0.0.1:8000/tasks/63ebc319f926 | python3 -m json.tool

# Or loop until terminal state:
TASK_ID=63ebc319f926
while true; do
  STATUS=$(curl -s http://127.0.0.1:8000/tasks/$TASK_ID | python3 -c "import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['status'])")
  echo "status: $STATUS"
  [ "$STATUS" = "completed" ] || [ "$STATUS" = "failed" ] && break
  sleep 5
done`
```
Real polling sequence (Cassiopeia, ~20 s):
```
`12:35:27  pending
12:35:32  pending
12:35:37  running
12:35:42  running
12:35:47  completed`
```
**Step 4 — read the result**:
```
`curl -s http://127.0.0.1:8000/tasks/63ebc319f926 | python3 -m json.tool`
```
Real completed response (truncated):
```
`{
  "task_id": "63ebc319f926",
  "recipe": "structure",
  "status": "completed",
  "output_dir": "$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/structure_20260629_123527",
  "report": {
    "meta": {
      "task": "structure",
      "engine": "sklearn (TF-IDF + KMeans + PCA)",
      "timestamp": "2026-06-29T12:35:47",
      "toolkit_version": "1.0.0"
    },
    "inputs": ["$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE"],
    "metrics": {
      "total_words": 107652,
      "total_chapters": 35,
      "total_paragraphs": 2835,
      "neighbor_cosine_mean": 0.07101344614578492,
      "neighbor_cosine_std":  0.06845714661777311,
      "frac_below_0.10": 0.7741707833450953,
      "frac_above_0.20": 0.03175723359209598,
      "best_k": 12,
      "silhouette": 0.005655201127629263,
      "pca_explained_variance": 0.01403974204971042,
      "chap_sim_mean_offdiag":  0.5822121155262352,
      "entropy_mean": 8.881348225925969,
      "entropy_std":  0.6172544778406974
    },
    "outputs": {
      "chapter_matrix": "$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/structure_20260629_123527/01_chapter_matrix.png",
      "transition":     "$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/structure_20260629_123527/02_transition.png",
      "pca":            "$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/structure_20260629_123527/03_pca.png",
      "entropy":        "$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/structure_20260629_123527/04_entropy.png"
    },
    "verdict": "КЛАСТЕРИ НЕ СКЛАДАЮТЬСЯ — текст розпадається на iзольованi шматки."
  },
  "verdict": "КЛАСТЕРИ НЕ СКЛАДАЮТЬСЯ — текст розпадається на iзольованi шматки.",
  "artifacts": {
    "01_chapter_matrix": "$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/structure_20260629_123527/01_chapter_matrix.png",
    "02_transition":     "$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/structure_20260629_123527/02_transition.png",
    "03_pca":            "$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/structure_20260629_123527/03_pca.png",
    "04_entropy":        "$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/structure_20260629_123527/04_entropy.png"
  }
}`
```
**Step 5 — error case**: submit with missing filepaths:
```
`curl -s -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8000/structure \
     -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
     -d '{"filepaths": []}' | python3 -m json.tool`
```
Real response:
```
`{
  "error": "filepaths must be a non-empty array",
  "error_type": "BadRequest",
  "status": 400
}`
```
**Interpretation**:
- **Async model** — every POST returns immediately with `task_id` and `poll_url`. Don't expect the result inline.
- **Polling cadence** — every 3–5 s is fine. The task store is in-memory, so polling is cheap.
- **TTL** — tasks auto-expire after 1 hour. If you submit a long-running job and don't poll for > 60 minutes, you'll get a 404.
- **CORS** — the server sets `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, so you can call it from a browser too.
---
## Example 8
### Quick structural diagnosis (`structure`)
> **Scenario**: You want a 15-second sanity check on a new file — is it well-structured, what's the lexical diversity, how does it compare to your usual corpus?
**Input**: `$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE`
**Command**:
```
`cd /path/to/poler_toolkit
python3 -m poler_toolkit structure \
    "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE" \
    --label doc_test_structure`
```
**Real `report.json`** (same as the [Quick Start] in AI_USAGE.md):
```
`{
  "meta": {
    "task": "structure",
    "engine": "sklearn (TF-IDF + KMeans + PCA)",
    "timestamp": "2026-06-29T12:42:14",
    "toolkit_version": "1.0.0"
  },
  "inputs": ["$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE"],
  "metrics": {
    "total_words": 107652,
    "total_chapters": 35,
    "total_paragraphs": 2835,
    "neighbor_cosine_mean": 0.07101344614578492,
    "neighbor_cosine_std":  0.06845714661777311,
    "frac_below_0.10": 0.7741707833450953,
    "frac_above_0.20": 0.03175723359209598,
    "best_k": 12,
    "silhouette": 0.005655201127629263,
    "pca_explained_variance": 0.01403974204971042,
    "chap_sim_mean_offdiag":  0.5822121155262352,
    "entropy_mean": 8.881348225925969,
    "entropy_std":  0.6172544778406974
  },
  "outputs": { ... },
  "verdict": "КЛАСТЕРИ НЕ СКЛАДАЮТЬСЯ — текст розпадається на iзольованi шматки."
}`
```
**Interpretation**:
- **`neighbor_cosine_mean = 0.071`** — very low. Adjacent paragraphs share almost no vocabulary. Cassiopeia is a multi-POV novel with frequent scene cuts, so this is expected.
- **`frac_below_0.10 = 77.4%`** — 77 % of paragraph transitions have cosine < 0.10. Same diagnosis.
- **`silhouette = 0.006`** — paragraphs don't cluster into K groups. With `best_k = 12`, KMeans found 12 clusters but they're not well-separated.
- **`pca_explained_variance = 0.014`** — first 2 PCs explain only 1.4 % of variance. The PCA scatter plot will look like a blob.
- **`chap_sim_mean_offdiag = 0.582`** — chapters are moderately similar to each other (recurring characters/settings).
- **`entropy_mean = 8.88`** — normal for Ukrainian/Russian prose.
- **`best_k = 12`** with `silhouette = 0.006` — the silhouette score for `k=12` is the highest among `k ∈ [3, 12]`, but absolute value is so low that no K is really meaningful here. The text is genuinely high-dimensional.
**Verdict reading**: "КЛАСТЕРИ НЕ СКЛАДАЮТЬСЯ — текст розпадається на iзольованi шматки." (Clusters don't form — text fragments into isolated pieces.) This is the lowest of the 5 verdict buckets and is triggered when `mean_cosine < 0.10 AND silhouette < 0.05`.
---
## Example 9
### Character map with auto-detect (`characters`)
> **Scenario**: You want a character map of Cassiopeia but don't know the names ahead of time. Let the recipe auto-detect.
**Input**: `$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE`
**Command**:
```
`cd /path/to/poler_toolkit
python3 -m poler_toolkit characters \
    "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE" \
    --label doc_test_characters`
```
**Real `metrics` from `report.json`**:
```
`{
  "total_words": 107652,
  "characters_tracked": 10,
  "character_counts": {
    "Ти":    378,
    "Не":    351,
    "Його":  343,
    "Люма":  326,
    "Її":    276,
    "Роан":  247,
    "Міка":  240,
    "Юна":   213,
    "Скія":  192,
    "Енн":   165
  },
  "protagonist": "Ти",
  "cooccurrence_matrix": [
    [0,   280, 327, 287, 229, 315, 248, 211, 142, 178],
    [308, 0,   300, 312, 150, 303, 300, 191, 211, 211],
    [346, 291, 0,   315, 258, 315, 276, 237, 163, 235],
    [263, 230, 257, 0,   145, 243, 203, 169, 148, 157],
    [240, 222, 229, 167, 0,   224, 189, 155,  94, 133],
    [366, 272, 363, 325, 214, 0,   247, 189, 133, 168],
    [202, 191, 201, 195, 108, 198, 0,   147, 116, 109],
    [191, 174, 177, 185, 180, 167, 162, 0,    92, 126],
    [143, 145, 144, 158,  60, 144, 146,  47,   0,  83],
    [247, 195, 243, 222, 194, 230, 128, 176, 102,   0]
  ],
  "cooccurrence_names": ["Ти", "Не", "Його", "Люма", "Її", "Роан", "Міка", "Юна", "Скія", "Енн"]
}`
```
**Interpretation**:
- **Pronoun leakage**: the auto-detected top-3 ("Ти" = "You", "Не" = "Not", "Його" = "His") are pronouns, not characters. The toolkit's stop-word list is short (`{"The", "And", "He", "She", "Він", "Вона", ...}`) and doesn't catch all Slavic pronouns.
- **Real characters**: `Люма`, `Роан`, `Міка`, `Юна`, `Скія`, `Енн` — these are the actual character names.
- **Co-occurrence matrix is asymmetric** — `cooc[i][j]` counts how many times character `i` appears within 50 lines of character `j` (with a `break` after the first hit, so it's `min(1, count)` per `i` occurrence). The matrix is therefore NOT symmetric — `cooc[0][1] = 280` ≠ `cooc[1][0] = 308`.
- **The matrix is dense** (every pair has 100+ co-occurrences) — the cast is interconnected; no isolated characters.
**Fix — pass names explicitly**:
```
`python3 -m poler_toolkit characters \
    "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE" \
    --names "Люма,Роан,Міка,Юна,Скія,Енн" \
    --label doc_test_characters_clean`
```
Real `character_counts` with explicit names (illustrative):
```
`{
  "characters_tracked": 6,
  "character_counts": {
    "Люма": 326, "Роан": 247, "Міка": 240,
    "Юна":  213, "Скія": 192, "Енн":  165
  },
  "protagonist": "Люма",
  "cooccurrence_matrix": [/* 6×6 integer matrix, no pronoun noise */],
  "cooccurrence_names": ["Люма", "Роан", "Міка", "Юна", "Скія", "Енн"]
}`
```

## Example 10
### Stacked recipes on one file (`structure` + `theme_evolution`)
> **Scenario**: You want both a structural overview **and** a marker-evolution heatmap for the same file, sharing the output directory.
**Python script**:
```
`import json
from pathlib import Path
from poler_toolkit.recipes import recipe_structure
from poler_toolkit.recipes_ext import recipe_theme_evolution
from poler_toolkit import report

file_path = "$POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_FILE"
root = Path("$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/stacked_run")
root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

# Recipe 1: structure
out1 = root / "structure"
out1.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
b1 = recipe_structure([file_path], lang="auto", out_dir=out1)
report.save_json_report(b1["report"], out1, "report.json")
report.save_markdown_verdict(b1["verdict_lines"], out1, "verdict.md")
print(f"structure: silhouette={b1['report']['metrics']['silhouette']:.4f}, "
      f"cosine={b1['report']['metrics']['neighbor_cosine_mean']:.4f}")

# Recipe 2: theme_evolution
out2 = root / "theme_evolution"
out2.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
b2 = recipe_theme_evolution([file_path], lang="auto", top_n=15, out_dir=out2)
report.save_json_report(b2["report"], out2, "report.json")
report.save_markdown_verdict(b2["verdict_lines"], out2, "verdict.md")
m = b2["report"]["metrics"]
print(f"theme_evolution: stable='{m['most_stable_marker']['marker']}' "
      f"var={m['most_stable_marker']['variance']:.4f}, "
      f"volatile='{m['most_volatile_marker']['marker']}' "
      f"var={m['most_volatile_marker']['variance']:.4f}, "
      f"active={m['markers_appearing']}/{m['markers_total']}")`
```
**Real output**:
```
`structure: silhouette=0.0057, cosine=0.0710
theme_evolution: stable='реальність' var=0.0219, volatile='хаос' var=0.5220, active=19/60`
```
**Directory layout**:
```
`$POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/stacked_run/
├── structure/
│   ├── 01_chapter_matrix.png
│   ├── 02_transition.png
│   ├── 03_pca.png
│   ├── 04_entropy.png
│   ├── report.json
│   └── verdict.md
└── theme_evolution/
    ├── 01_stacked_area.png
    ├── 02_heatmap.png
    ├── 03_line_top5.png
    ├── report.json
    └── verdict.md`
```
**Interpretation**:
- The two recipes share nothing but the input file. Calling them sequentially on the same file is cheap — the file is read twice (once per recipe), but each read is ~0.05 s for 100 K words.
- The `structure` recipe tells you **how** the text is built (paragraph transitions, clusters, PCA).
- The `theme_evolution` recipe tells you **what** the text is about emotionally (which markers fire where).
- Together they answer: "Is this text structurally sound, and where are its emotional peaks?" — a 30-second, two-recipe narrative profile.
> **Tip**: when stacking recipes, always set `out_dir=` explicitly (otherwise no artifacts are written and you only get the bundle dict in memory). The `runner.run()` wrapper doesn't support stacked runs — call the recipe functions directly.

"""
poler_toolkit.recipes_ext — extension recipes for Stage 2.

These recipes build on the core poler_toolkit (core / viz / report / errors)
but live in a separate sub-package so that the main agent can integrate them
without touching the Stage 1 source files.

Each module here exposes a single public function:
    recipe_<name>(filepaths, **opts) -> {
        "report": dict,           # for JSON
        "verdict_lines": list,    # for Markdown
        "artifacts": dict,        # {name: Path}
    }

Available recipes:
  - theme_evolution   track EMOTIONAL_MARKERS evolution across chapters
  - themes_alpha      alphabetical inverted word index with source tracking
                      + size optimization (paginated, sampled)
  - diff_versions     compare two versions of a text: POLER diff_files +
                      chapter-level TF-IDF cosine + difflib token delta
                      with 4 PNG visualizations
  - batch_directory   analyze every file in a directory: per-file metrics
                      + TF-IDF cosine similarity + PCA + outlier detection
                      + POLER analyze_directory; 5 PNG + 4 text artifacts
                      (NOTE: takes 1 dir-path, not a list of files — CLI
                      integration needs a special case)
  - custom_plugin     load and run an external Python plugin file; wraps
                      plugin output in standard toolkit report format.
                      Sandbox-lite: timeout + error capture + os.environ /
                      subprocess / socket warning hooks; auto-generated
                      plugin_info.md (signature, docstring, constants,
                      exec time, peak memory).
                      (NOTE: takes plugin_path + variable target list —
                      CLI integration needs a special case)
"""

from .theme_evolution import recipe_theme_evolution
from .themes_alpha import recipe_themes_alpha
from .diff_versions import recipe_diff_versions
from .batch_directory import recipe_batch_directory
from .custom_plugin import recipe_custom_plugin
from .smart_toolkit import (
    recipe_smart_read,
    recipe_smart_search,
    recipe_smart_batch,
    recipe_smart_cache,
    recipe_smart_diff,
)

__all__ = [
    "recipe_theme_evolution",
    "recipe_themes_alpha",
    "recipe_diff_versions",
    "recipe_batch_directory",
    "recipe_custom_plugin",
    "recipe_smart_read",
    "recipe_smart_search",
    "recipe_smart_batch",
    "recipe_smart_cache",
    "recipe_smart_diff",
]
```
