```
"""
poler_toolkit.recipes_ext.batch_directory
=========================================

Recipe: analyse **every file in a directory** and produce a directory-level
overview with per-file metrics, cross-file similarity, PCA projection,
outlier detection, and POLER's own ``analyze_directory`` result.

POLER[n] framing
----------------
* **℘ (Pattern)** — directory-level aggregation: 50 files at once, not 50×1.
* **O (Operational)** — user has a folder of files, wants overview +
  per-file metrics + outliers.
* **L (Logic)** — uses ``poler_v6.analyze_directory`` for the core analysis
  + cross-file TF-IDF cosine + PCA + outlier detection on top.
* **ε (Energy)** — focused on cross-file comparison + outlier surfacing.
* **R[n] (Resonance)** — multi-level: per-file report + aggregated dashboard
  + outlier list.

What the recipe does
--------------------
1. Validate that ``filepaths`` is a single-element list whose only entry
   is a directory path. (Stage 2 contract: recipes take a list, so the
   directory is wrapped in a 1-element list for CLI / runner
   compatibility. The CLI will need a special case — see integration
   notes at the bottom of this docstring.)
2. Walk the directory (optionally recursive, optionally filtered by
   extension) and read every text file.
3. For each file compute basic metrics:
   * word count, char count, line count
   * Shannon entropy (bits/token)
   * average word length
   * top-5 most frequent words
4. Build a cross-file TF-IDF + cosine similarity matrix (N × N).
5. Project files to 2-D via PCA for a quick visual map.
6. Detect outliers: files whose maximum cosine similarity to *any*
   other file is below ``OUTLIER_THRESHOLD`` (0.20).
7. Call ``poler_v6.analyze_directory(dir_path, keyword, ...)`` via
   ``core.safe_call``. POLER returns per-file POLER ε/fragment counts
   plus cross-file clusters.
8. Call ``poler_v6.format_directory_markdown(result)`` via
   ``core.safe_call`` and save the output verbatim as
   ``poler_directory.md``.
9. Render five PNG artifacts:
   * ``01_file_similarity.png`` — N × N heatmap of TF-IDF cosine
     similarity between files.
   * ``02_pca_files.png``      — 2-D PCA projection, each file labelled.
   * ``03_wordcounts.png``     — bar chart of word counts, sorted desc.
   * ``04_entropy_dist.png``   — histogram of Shannon entropy values.
   * ``05_outliers_radar.png`` — radar chart of normalised metrics for
     the top-3 outliers (most isolated files).
10. Emit the standard poler_toolkit bundle:
    * ``per_file_report.json`` — full per-file metrics (machine-readable).
    * ``poler_directory.md``   — POLER's ``format_directory_markdown``
      output.
    * ``report.json``          — via ``report.wrap_report``.
    * ``verdict.md``           — via ``report.verdict_block`` +
      ``metric_table``.

Function signature
------------------
::

    def recipe_batch_directory(
        filepaths: list,            # 1-element list: [dir_path]
        *,
        keyword: Optional[str] = None,
        extensions: Optional[list] = None,  # default ['.txt', '.md']
        recursive: bool = True,
        top_n: int = 10,
        out_dir: Path = None,
    ) -> dict:
        # Returns {"report": dict, "verdict_lines": list, "artifacts": dict}

RECIPES dict entry (for main agent)
-----------------------------------
::

    "batch_directory": {
        "fn": recipe_batch_directory,   # import from .recipes_ext.batch_directory
        "n_files": 1,                   # SPECIAL: dir path, not a file
        "description": "Analyze every file in a directory: per-file metrics + "
                       "TF-IDF cosine similarity + PCA + outlier detection + "
                       "POLER analyze_directory; 5 PNG + 4 text artifacts",
        "options": ["keyword", "extensions", "recursive", "top_n"],
    }

CLI integration note
--------------------
This recipe takes **one directory path** (not a list of files). The main
agent's CLI dispatcher will need a special case: if the recipe is
``batch_directory``, the user-supplied path should be treated as a
directory and passed as ``[dir_path]``. Alternatively the CLI may pass
``[args.path]`` directly and let the recipe validate. Either way the
recipe expects ``filepaths[0]`` to be an existing directory.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import logging
import math
import re
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

import numpy as np
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
from sklearn.metrics.pairwise import cosine_similarity
from sklearn.decomposition import PCA

# Recipe lives in poler_toolkit.recipes_ext, so parent-of-parent is
# poler_toolkit itself. Primary path: relative imports (matches Stage 2
# integration contract). Fallback: absolute imports, so the file can
# also be invoked directly as
# ``python3 .../batch_directory.py DIR`` for testing.
try:
    from .. import core
    from .. import report
    from .. import viz
    from ..errors import (
        RecipeError, ConfigurationError, PolerEngineError, FileError,
    )
except ImportError:  # pragma: no cover - script-mode fallback
    import os as _os
    import sys as _sys
    _here = _os.path.dirname(_os.path.abspath(__file__))
    _scripts = _os.path.dirname(_os.path.dirname(_here))
    if _scripts not in _sys.path:
        _sys.path.insert(0, _scripts)
    from poler_toolkit import core  # type: ignore
    from poler_toolkit import report  # type: ignore
    from poler_toolkit import viz  # type: ignore
    from poler_toolkit.errors import (  # type: ignore
        RecipeError, ConfigurationError, PolerEngineError, FileError,
    )

log = logging.getLogger("poler_toolkit.recipes_ext.batch_directory")


# ============================================================
# Tunable constants
# ============================================================
DEFAULT_EXTENSIONS = [".txt", ".md"]
"""File extensions scanned by default. Pass ``extensions=`` to override."""

OUTLIER_THRESHOLD = 0.20
"""A file is an *outlier* if its maximum cosine similarity to any other
file is below this value. The recipe surfaces the top-N outliers (by
ascending max-similarity) in the radar chart and the verdict."""

TOP_WORDS_PER_FILE = 5
"""How many most-frequent words to record per file (spec: 5)."""

_TFIDF_MAX_FEATURES = 8000
"""Cap on TF-IDF vocabulary so the cosine matrix builds fast even on a
directory with hundreds of moderately-sized files."""

_PCA_N_COMPONENTS = 2
"""PCA projection dimensionality (always 2 for the scatter plot)."""


# ============================================================
# Tokenizer (kept consistent with core.auto_detect_keyword and the
# other recipes_ext modules so counts are directly comparable).
# ============================================================
TOKEN_RE = re.compile(r"[\w’']+")


def _tokenize(text: str) -> List[str]:
    return TOKEN_RE.findall(text.lower())


def _shannon_entropy(tokens: List[str]) -> float:
    """Shannon entropy in bits/token. Empty input → 0.0."""
    if not tokens:
        return 0.0
    counts: Dict[str, int] = {}
    for t in tokens:
        counts[t] = counts.get(t, 0) + 1
    total = len(tokens)
    h = 0.0
    for c in counts.values():
        p = c / total
        h -= p * math.log2(p)
    return h


# ============================================================
# Local visualization helpers
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# Stage 2 constraint: do NOT modify viz.py. The existing
# ``viz.chapter_matrix`` builds a square N×N heatmap but hardcodes the
# axis label as "Глава" (chapter), which is wrong for files. The other
# four charts (PCA with labels, sorted word-count bar, entropy histogram,
# outlier radar) don't exist in viz.py at all. Implement them locally so
# viz.py stays untouched for the main agent.
# ============================================================
def _safe_savefig(fig, path: Path) -> None:
    """Save + close. Honours toolkit DPI.

    Catches any exception, logs a warning and re-raises as
    ``VisualizationError`` so the caller can decide whether to abort
    the whole recipe or just skip this one PNG. (We close the figure
    in a ``finally`` block so memory is freed even when savefig fails.)
    """
    try:
        fig.savefig(path, dpi=viz.DPI)
        log.info(f"Saved: {path}")
    except Exception as e:
        raise viz.VisualizationError(f"savefig failed: {e}") from e
    finally:
        plt.close(fig)


def _cap_fig_size(w: float, h: float, max_dim: float = 22.0) -> Tuple[float, float]:
    """Cap a figure's dimensions to ``max_dim`` inches per side while
    preserving aspect ratio. Stops matplotlib from trying to allocate a
    260-inch-wide canvas when a directory has hundreds of files."""
    scale = min(1.0, max_dim / max(w, h, 1.0))
    return (w * scale, h * scale)


def _short_name(path: Path, max_len: int = 18) -> str:
    """Stem of a path, truncated for axis labels."""
    s = path.stem
    if len(s) <= max_len:
        return s
    return s[: max_len - 1] + "\u2026"


def _file_similarity_heatmap(
    sim_matrix: np.ndarray,
    file_labels: List[str],
    out_path: Path,
    title: str = "File similarity (TF-IDF cosine)",
) -> None:
    """N × N heatmap of pairwise cosine similarity between files.

    Annotates cells with the similarity value when N is small enough for
    the numbers to remain legible (≤ 20). For larger matrices the tick
    labels are hidden — they would be unreadable anyway — and the figure
    size is capped so savefig doesn't try to allocate a 260-inch canvas.
    """
    n = len(file_labels)
    fig_w = max(8, n * 0.55 + 2)
    fig_h = max(7, n * 0.55 + 2)
    fig_w, fig_h = _cap_fig_size(fig_w, fig_h, max_dim=22.0)
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(fig_w, fig_h), constrained_layout=True)
    im = ax.imshow(sim_matrix, cmap="viridis", vmin=0, vmax=1, aspect="equal")
    ax.set_title(title, fontsize=13, pad=10)
    if n <= 60:
        ax.set_xticks(range(n))
        ax.set_yticks(range(n))
        fs = max(5, 10 - n // 12)
        ax.set_xticklabels(file_labels, rotation=45, ha="right", fontsize=fs)
        ax.set_yticklabels(file_labels, fontsize=fs)
    else:
        # Too many files — show only a sparse set of ticks.
        step = max(1, n // 20)
        idxs = list(range(0, n, step))
        ax.set_xticks(idxs)
        ax.set_yticks(idxs)
        ax.set_xticklabels([file_labels[i] for i in idxs],
                           rotation=45, ha="right", fontsize=7)
        ax.set_yticklabels([file_labels[i] for i in idxs], fontsize=7)
    ax.set_xlabel("File")
    ax.set_ylabel("File")
    fig.colorbar(im, ax=ax, fraction=0.046, pad=0.02, label="cosine sim")
    # Annotate cells with the value only when the matrix isn't too big.
    if n <= 20:
        for i in range(n):
            for j in range(n):
                v = float(sim_matrix[i, j])
                color = "white" if v < 0.55 else "black"
                ax.text(j, i, f"{v:.2f}", ha="center", va="center",
                        fontsize=max(5, 9 - n // 4), color=color)
    _safe_savefig(fig, out_path)


def _pca_files_scatter(
    coords: np.ndarray,
    file_labels: List[str],
    out_path: Path,
    explained_variance: Optional[Tuple[float, float]] = None,
    outlier_idx: Optional[List[int]] = None,
    title: str = "PCA projection of files",
) -> None:
    """2-D PCA scatter with each file labelled.

    Outliers (if any) are highlighted in red and enlarged so they're
    easy to spot. When the directory has many files (>40) only the
    outliers are text-labelled — labelling every point would just
    produce an unreadable cloud.
    """
    n = len(file_labels)
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(12, 8), constrained_layout=True)
    outlier_set = set(outlier_idx or [])
    if n > 0:
        xs_all = coords[:, 0]
        ys_all = coords[:, 1]
        normal_idx = [i for i in range(n) if i not in outlier_set]
        out_idx = [i for i in range(n) if i in outlier_set]
        dot_size = 120 if n <= 50 else max(20, 120 - (n - 50) // 5)
        if normal_idx:
            ax.scatter(
                xs_all[normal_idx], ys_all[normal_idx],
                c=viz.COLOR_PRIMARY, s=dot_size, alpha=0.65,
                edgecolors="black", linewidths=0.4,
                label="file",
                zorder=3,
            )
        if out_idx:
            ax.scatter(
                xs_all[out_idx], ys_all[out_idx],
                c=viz.COLOR_ACCENT, s=260, alpha=0.95,
                edgecolors="black", linewidths=1.0,
                marker="*", label="outlier",
                zorder=4,
            )
    # Labels: always for outliers, only for normal points when n ≤ 40.
    label_all = n <= 40
    for i, lbl in enumerate(file_labels):
        if i in outlier_set or label_all:
            ax.annotate(
                lbl, (coords[i, 0], coords[i, 1]),
                xytext=(5, 5), textcoords="offset points",
                fontsize=8, alpha=0.9,
            )
    if explained_variance:
        ax.set_xlabel(f"PC1 ({explained_variance[0]:.1%})")
        ax.set_ylabel(f"PC2 ({explained_variance[1]:.1%})")
    else:
        ax.set_xlabel("PC1")
        ax.set_ylabel("PC2")
    ax.set_title(title, fontsize=13, pad=10)
    ax.grid(alpha=0.25)
    if n > 0:
        ax.legend(loc="best", fontsize=9)
    _safe_savefig(fig, out_path)


def _wordcounts_bar(
    file_labels: List[str],
    word_counts: List[int],
    out_path: Path,
    title: str = "Word count per file (sorted)",
    max_bars: int = 50,
) -> None:
    """Horizontal bar chart of word counts, sorted descending.

    Horizontal bars because file names are usually long; sorting makes
    the distribution immediately readable. When the directory has more
    than ``max_bars`` files only the top ``max_bars`` are shown (the
    rest would be invisible anyway at the bottom of a 470-row chart).
    """
    n = len(file_labels)
    if n == 0:
        file_labels = ["(no files)"]
        word_counts = [0]
        n = 1
    # Sort descending by word count.
    order = sorted(range(n), key=lambda i: -word_counts[i])
    truncated = len(order) > max_bars
    if truncated:
        order = order[:max_bars]
    labels = [file_labels[i] for i in order]
    values = [word_counts[i] for i in order]
    n_show = len(labels)
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(max(9, n_show * 0.5 + 4),
                                     max(5, n_show * 0.35 + 2)),
                            constrained_layout=True)
    bars = ax.barh(range(n_show), values,
                   color=viz.COLOR_SECONDARY, alpha=0.85,
                   edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.4)
    ax.invert_yaxis()  # largest on top
    ax.set_yticks(range(n_show))
    ax.set_yticklabels(labels, fontsize=8)
    ax.set_xlabel("Word count (tokens)")
    suffix = f" (top {max_bars} of {n})" if truncated else ""
    ax.set_title(f"{title}{suffix}", fontsize=13, pad=10)
    if values:
        m = max(values)
        for i, v in enumerate(values):
            ax.text(v + m * 0.005 + 0.5, i, f"{int(v)}",
                    va="center", fontsize=8)
        ax.set_xlim(0, m * 1.12 + 1)
    ax.grid(alpha=0.25, axis="x")
    _safe_savefig(fig, out_path)


def _entropy_hist(
    entropies: List[float],
    out_path: Path,
    title: str = "Shannon entropy distribution",
    bins: int = 10,
) -> None:
    """Histogram of per-file Shannon entropy values.

    Overlays the mean and ±1σ reference lines so the spread is visible
    at a glance.
    """
    arr = np.array(entropies, dtype=np.float64) if entropies else np.array([0.0])
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(11, 5), constrained_layout=True)
    n_bins = max(1, min(bins, len(arr)))
    ax.hist(arr, bins=n_bins, color=viz.COLOR_PRIMARY, alpha=0.80,
            edgecolor="black", linewidth=0.5)
    if len(arr) > 0:
        m = float(arr.mean())
        ax.axvline(m, color=viz.COLOR_ACCENT, linestyle="--", linewidth=1.5,
                   label=f"mean={m:.2f}")
        if len(arr) > 1:
            s = float(arr.std())
            ax.axvline(m - s, color=viz.COLOR_WARN, linestyle=":",
                       linewidth=1.1, label=f"−1σ={m - s:.2f}")
            ax.axvline(m + s, color=viz.COLOR_OK, linestyle=":",
                       linewidth=1.1, label=f"+1σ={m + s:.2f}")
    ax.set_xlabel("Shannon entropy (bits/token)")
    ax.set_ylabel("Number of files")
    ax.set_title(title, fontsize=13, pad=10)
    ax.legend(loc="best", fontsize=9)
    ax.grid(alpha=0.25, axis="y")
    _safe_savefig(fig, out_path)


def _outliers_radar(
    outlier_metrics: List[Dict[str, Any]],
    out_path: Path,
    title: str = "Outlier radar — normalised metrics",
) -> None:
    """Radar chart comparing the top-N outlier files on six normalised
    metrics.

    Each entry in ``outlier_metrics`` must contain the keys::

        {"label", "word_count_n", "char_count_n", "line_count_n",
         "entropy_n", "avg_word_len_n", "isolation_n"}

    where every ``*_n`` value is in [0, 1] (already normalised against
    the full population, *not* just the outlier subset). ``isolation_n``
    is ``1 - max_cosine_sim_to_others`` so a value of 1 means the file
    has zero cosine overlap with every other file in the directory.
    """
    axes_labels = [
        "Word count",
        "Char count",
        "Line count",
        "Entropy",
        "Avg word len",
        "Isolation",
    ]
    n_axes = len(axes_labels)
    angles = np.linspace(0, 2 * np.pi, n_axes, endpoint=False).tolist()
    angles += angles[:1]  # close the loop

    n_out = len(outlier_metrics)
    if n_out == 0:
        outlier_metrics = [{
            "label": "(no outliers)",
            "word_count_n": 0.0, "char_count_n": 0.0,
            "line_count_n": 0.0, "entropy_n": 0.0,
            "avg_word_len_n": 0.0, "isolation_n": 0.0,
        }]
        n_out = 1

    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(9, 9), subplot_kw={"projection": "polar"},
                           constrained_layout=True)
    colors = plt.cm.tab10(np.linspace(0, 0.85, n_out))
    for i, m in enumerate(outlier_metrics):
        values = [
            float(m.get("word_count_n", 0.0)),
            float(m.get("char_count_n", 0.0)),
            float(m.get("line_count_n", 0.0)),
            float(m.get("entropy_n", 0.0)),
            float(m.get("avg_word_len_n", 0.0)),
            float(m.get("isolation_n", 0.0)),
        ]
        values += values[:1]
        ax.plot(angles, values, color=colors[i], linewidth=1.8,
                label=m.get("label", f"file {i + 1}"))
        ax.fill(angles, values, color=colors[i], alpha=0.18)

    ax.set_theta_offset(np.pi / 2)
    ax.set_theta_direction(-1)
    ax.set_xticks(angles[:-1])
    ax.set_xticklabels(axes_labels, fontsize=10)
    ax.set_ylim(0, 1.0)
    ax.set_yticks([0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0])
    ax.set_yticklabels(["0.25", "0.50", "0.75", "1.00"], fontsize=8)
    ax.grid(alpha=0.4)
    ax.set_title(title, fontsize=13, pad=18)
    ax.legend(loc="upper right", bbox_to_anchor=(1.25, 1.10), fontsize=9)
    _safe_savefig(fig, out_path)


# ============================================================
# Directory walking
# ============================================================
def _walk_directory(
    dir_path: Path,
    extensions: List[str],
    recursive: bool,
) -> List[Path]:
    """Return a sorted list of files in ``dir_path``.

    Respects the ``recursive`` flag (top-level only if False) and the
    extension filter (case-insensitive suffix match, so ``.MD`` matches
    ``.md``).
    """
    exts = [e.lower() for e in extensions]
    # Normalize so leading-dot is consistent.
    exts = [e if e.startswith(".") else "." + e for e in exts]

    iterator = dir_path.rglob("*") if recursive else dir_path.glob("*")
    files: List[Path] = []
    for p in iterator:
        if not p.is_file():
            continue
        suffix = p.suffix.lower()
        # Handle compound suffixes (.tar.gz) too — match end of name.
        name_lower = p.name.lower()
        if any(name_lower.endswith(e) for e in exts):
            files.append(p)
    files.sort()
    return files


# ============================================================
# Per-file metrics
# ============================================================
def _per_file_metrics(path: Path) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Compute basic metrics for a single text file.

    Returns a dict with keys::

        {"path", "name", "word_count", "char_count", "line_count",
         "shannon_entropy", "avg_word_length", "top_words",
         "read_error": None|str}

    ``top_words`` is a list of ``[word, count]`` pairs (JSON-friendly).
    """
    out: Dict[str, Any] = {
        "path": str(path),
        "name": path.name,
        "word_count": 0,
        "char_count": 0,
        "line_count": 0,
        "shannon_entropy": 0.0,
        "avg_word_length": 0.0,
        "top_words": [],
        "read_error": None,
    }
    try:
        text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8-sig", errors="replace")
    except Exception as e:
        out["read_error"] = f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}"
        log.warning(f"  could not read {path}: {e}")
        return out
    text = text.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace("\r", "\n").replace("\x00", "")
    tokens = _tokenize(text)
    n_words = len(tokens)
    n_chars = len(text)
    n_lines = text.count("\n") + (1 if text and not text.endswith("\n") else 0)
    entropy = _shannon_entropy(tokens)
    avg_wl = (sum(len(w) for w in tokens) / n_words) if n_words else 0.0
    # Top-5 most frequent words.
    counts: Dict[str, int] = {}
    for t in tokens:
        counts[t] = counts.get(t, 0) + 1
    top = sorted(counts.items(), key=lambda kv: (-kv[1], kv[0]))[:TOP_WORDS_PER_FILE]
    out.update({
        "word_count": n_words,
        "char_count": n_chars,
        "line_count": n_lines,
        "shannon_entropy": round(entropy, 4),
        "avg_word_length": round(avg_wl, 4),
        "top_words": [[w, c] for w, c in top],
    })
    return out


# ============================================================
# Main recipe
# ============================================================
def recipe_batch_directory(
    filepaths: list,
    *,
    keyword: Optional[str] = None,
    extensions: Optional[list] = None,
    recursive: bool = True,
    top_n: int = 10,
    out_dir: Path = None,
) -> dict:
    """Analyse every file in a directory.

    See module docstring for the full description.

    Returns
    -------
    dict
        ``{"report": dict, "verdict_lines": list, "artifacts": dict}`` —
        the standard poler_toolkit bundle.
    """
    # ----- 1. validate inputs -----------------------------------------
    if not isinstance(filepaths, (list, tuple)) or len(filepaths) != 1:
        raise ConfigurationError(
            "batch_directory expects exactly 1 entry in `filepaths` "
            "(the directory path)",
            hint="Pass [dir_path] — a single-element list with the "
                 "directory you want to analyse.",
        )
    dir_path = Path(filepaths[0])
    if not dir_path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"directory not found: {dir_path}")
    if not dir_path.is_dir():
        raise ConfigurationError(
            f"not a directory: {dir_path}",
            hint="batch_directory requires a directory path, not a file.",
        )
    exts = list(extensions) if extensions else list(DEFAULT_EXTENSIONS)
    log.info(f"[batch_directory] dir={dir_path}  exts={exts}  "
             f"recursive={recursive}  top_n={top_n}")

    # ----- 2. walk directory ------------------------------------------
    files = _walk_directory(dir_path, exts, recursive)
    n_files = len(files)
    log.info(f"  found {n_files} files")
    if n_files == 0:
        raise RecipeError(
            f"no files matching {exts} found in {dir_path}"
            f"{' (recursive)' if recursive else ''}",
            hint="Try --extensions .txt .md or remove --no-recursive.",
        )

    # ----- 3. per-file metrics ----------------------------------------
    per_file: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
    readable_files: List[Path] = []
    readable_texts: List[str] = []
    for fp in files:
        m = _per_file_metrics(fp)
        per_file.append(m)
        if m["read_error"] is None:
            readable_files.append(fp)
            readable_texts.append(
                Path(fp).read_text(encoding="utf-8-sig", errors="replace")
                .replace("\r\n", "\n").replace("\r", "\n").replace("\x00", "")
            )
    n_readable = len(readable_files)
    log.info(f"  readable: {n_readable}/{n_files}")

    # ----- 4. cross-file TF-IDF cosine similarity ---------------------
    sim_matrix = np.zeros((n_readable, n_readable), dtype=np.float64)
    if n_readable >= 1:
        try:
            vec = TfidfVectorizer(
                max_features=_TFIDF_MAX_FEATURES,
                token_pattern=r"(?u)\b[\w’']+\b",
                lowercase=True,
                sublinear_tf=True,
            )
            X = vec.fit_transform(readable_texts)
            sim_matrix = cosine_similarity(X)
            # Zero out the diagonal (a file is always 1.0 to itself; we
            # don't want self-similarity to mask outliers).
            np.fill_diagonal(sim_matrix, 0.0)
        except Exception as e:
            log.warning(f"TF-IDF cosine similarity failed: {e}")
            sim_matrix = np.zeros((n_readable, n_readable), dtype=np.float64)

    # ----- 5. PCA 2-D projection --------------------------------------
    pca_coords = np.zeros((n_readable, 2), dtype=np.float64)
    pca_explained: Tuple[float, float] = (0.0, 0.0)
    if n_readable >= 2:
        try:
            pca = PCA(n_components=_PCA_N_COMPONENTS, random_state=42)
            proj = pca.fit_transform(X.toarray())
            pca_coords = proj
            ev = pca.explained_variance_ratio_
            if len(ev) >= 2:
                pca_explained = (float(ev[0]), float(ev[1]))
        except Exception as e:
            log.warning(f"PCA failed: {e}")
            pca_coords = np.zeros((n_readable, 2), dtype=np.float64)

    # ----- 6. outlier detection ---------------------------------------
    # For each file i, find the max cosine similarity to any *other*
    # file. Files whose max-sim is below OUTLIER_THRESHOLD are flagged
    # as outliers. Sort ascending by max-sim (most isolated first).
    outliers: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
    per_file_max_sim: List[float] = []
    for i in range(n_readable):
        if n_readable <= 1:
            max_sim = 0.0
        else:
            row = sim_matrix[i]
            # row[i] is already 0 (zeroed diagonal), so max over the
            # rest of the row gives the best non-self similarity.
            max_sim = float(row.max()) if row.size else 0.0
        per_file_max_sim.append(max_sim)
        is_outlier = (n_readable > 1) and (max_sim < OUTLIER_THRESHOLD)
        if is_outlier:
            outliers.append({
                "name": readable_files[i].name,
                "path": str(readable_files[i]),
                "max_cosine_to_other": round(max_sim, 4),
                "word_count": per_file[i]["word_count"],
                "char_count": per_file[i]["char_count"],
                "shannon_entropy": per_file[i]["shannon_entropy"],
                "avg_word_length": per_file[i]["avg_word_length"],
            })
    outliers.sort(key=lambda o: o["max_cosine_to_other"])
    top_outliers = outliers[:3]

    # ----- 7. POLER analyze_directory ---------------------------------
    chosen_keyword = keyword
    if not chosen_keyword:
        # Auto-detect keyword from combined text of all readable files.
        combined = "\n".join(readable_texts)
        try:
            chosen_keyword = core.pick_keyword(combined, hint=None)
            log.info(f"  auto-detected keyword: '{chosen_keyword}'")
        except Exception as e:
            log.warning(f"  auto-detect keyword failed ({e}); "
                        f"falling back to ''")
            chosen_keyword = ""

    poler_dir_result: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
    poler_dir_error: Optional[str] = None
    try:
        poler_dir_result = core.safe_call(
            "analyze_directory",
            str(dir_path),
            chosen_keyword,
            top_n=int(top_n),
            extensions=exts,
            include_images=False,
            quiet=True,
        )
        log.info(
            f"  POLER: scanned={poler_dir_result.get('files_scanned', 0)}, "
            f"with_hits={poler_dir_result.get('files_with_hits', 0)}, "
            f"total_windows={poler_dir_result.get('total_windows', 0)}"
        )
    except PolerEngineError as e:
        log.warning(f"POLER analyze_directory failed: {e}")
        poler_dir_error = str(e)

    # ----- 8. POLER format_directory_markdown -------------------------
    poler_md = ""
    if poler_dir_result is not None:
        try:
            poler_md = core.safe_call(
                "format_directory_markdown", poler_dir_result,
            )
        except PolerEngineError as e:
            log.warning(f"format_directory_markdown failed: {e}")
            poler_md = f"<!-- format_directory_markdown failed: {e} -->\n"
    else:
        poler_md = (
            f"<!-- POLER analyze_directory failed: {poler_dir_error} -->\n"
        )

    # ----- 9. aggregates ----------------------------------------------
    word_counts = [m["word_count"] for m in per_file]
    char_counts = [m["char_count"] for m in per_file]
    line_counts = [m["line_count"] for m in per_file]
    entropies = [m["shannon_entropy"] for m in per_file]
    avg_wls = [m["avg_word_length"] for m in per_file]
    wc_arr = np.array(word_counts, dtype=np.float64)
    ent_arr = np.array(entropies, dtype=np.float64)

    aggregates: Dict[str, Any] = {
        "file_count": n_files,
        "readable_count": n_readable,
        "total_words": int(sum(word_counts)),
        "total_chars": int(sum(char_counts)),
        "total_lines": int(sum(line_counts)),
        "mean_word_count": round(float(wc_arr.mean()), 2) if n_files else 0.0,
        "std_word_count": round(float(wc_arr.std()), 2) if n_files else 0.0,
        "min_word_count": int(wc_arr.min()) if n_files else 0,
        "max_word_count": int(wc_arr.max()) if n_files else 0,
        "mean_entropy": round(float(ent_arr.mean()), 4) if n_files else 0.0,
        "std_entropy": round(float(ent_arr.std()), 4) if n_files else 0.0,
        "min_entropy": round(float(ent_arr.min()), 4) if n_files else 0.0,
        "max_entropy": round(float(ent_arr.max()), 4) if n_files else 0.0,
        "mean_avg_word_length":
            round(float(np.mean(avg_wls)), 4) if n_files else 0.0,
        "outlier_count": len(outliers),
        "outlier_threshold": OUTLIER_THRESHOLD,
    }

    # ----- 10. normalised outlier metrics (for radar) -----------------
    # Build a normaliser over the *whole* population (so an outlier
    # showing 1.0 on a given axis means it has the max value of that
    # metric across every file in the directory, not just among
    # outliers).
    def _norm(val: float, vals: List[float]) -> float:
        if not vals:
            return 0.0
        lo = min(vals)
        hi = max(vals)
        if hi - lo < 1e-9:
            return 0.0
        return float((val - lo) / (hi - lo))

    outlier_radar_metrics: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
    for o in top_outliers:
        # find the per-file dict for this outlier
        idx = next(
            (i for i, m in enumerate(per_file)
             if m["name"] == o["name"]),
            None,
        )
        if idx is None:
            continue
        m = per_file[idx]
        max_sim = per_file_max_sim[idx] if idx < len(per_file_max_sim) else 0.0
        outlier_radar_metrics.append({
            "label": _short_name(Path(m["path"])),
            "word_count_n": _norm(m["word_count"], word_counts),
            "char_count_n": _norm(m["char_count"], char_counts),
            "line_count_n": _norm(m["line_count"], line_counts),
            "entropy_n": _norm(m["shannon_entropy"], entropies),
            "avg_word_len_n": _norm(m["avg_word_length"], avg_wls),
            "isolation_n": round(1.0 - max_sim, 4),
        })

    # ----- 11. metrics dict (for report.json) -------------------------
    # Reduce the per-file list to JSON-friendly primitives (already the
    # case) and store the full similarity matrix as a list-of-lists.
    sim_matrix_out: List[List[float]] = (
        [[round(float(sim_matrix[i, j]), 4) for j in range(n_readable)]
         for i in range(n_readable)]
        if n_readable > 0 else []
    )
    pca_coords_out: List[List[float]] = (
        [[round(float(pca_coords[i, 0]), 4),
          round(float(pca_coords[i, 1]), 4)]
         for i in range(n_readable)]
        if n_readable > 0 else []
    )

    poler_summary: Dict[str, Any] = {
        "_error": poler_dir_error,
    }
    if poler_dir_result is not None:
        poler_summary.update({
            "keyword": poler_dir_result.get("keyword", chosen_keyword),
            "directory": poler_dir_result.get("directory", str(dir_path)),
            "files_scanned": int(poler_dir_result.get("files_scanned", 0)),
            "files_with_hits": int(poler_dir_result.get("files_with_hits", 0)),
            "total_windows": int(poler_dir_result.get("total_windows", 0)),
            "cross_resonance": bool(poler_dir_result.get("cross_resonance", False)),
            "cross_clusters_count": len(poler_dir_result.get("cross_clusters", [])),
            "top_by_epsilon_count": len(poler_dir_result.get("top_by_epsilon", [])),
        })

    metrics: Dict[str, Any] = {
        "directory": str(dir_path),
        "extensions": exts,
        "recursive": bool(recursive),
        "keyword": chosen_keyword,
        "aggregates": aggregates,
        "per_file": per_file,
        "per_file_max_sim": [round(s, 4) for s in per_file_max_sim],
        "cross_file": {
            "similarity_matrix_shape": [n_readable, n_readable],
            "similarity_matrix": sim_matrix_out,
            "pca_explained_variance": list(pca_explained),
            "pca_coords": pca_coords_out,
            "outlier_threshold": OUTLIER_THRESHOLD,
            "outliers": outliers,
            "top_outliers": top_outliers,
        },
        "poler_directory": poler_summary,
        "config": {
            "extensions": exts,
            "recursive": bool(recursive),
            "top_n": int(top_n),
            "keyword": chosen_keyword,
            "outlier_threshold": OUTLIER_THRESHOLD,
            "top_words_per_file": TOP_WORDS_PER_FILE,
            "tfidf_max_features": _TFIDF_MAX_FEATURES,
        },
    }

    # ----- 12. write outputs (only if out_dir given) ------------------
    artifacts: Dict[str, Path] = {}
    written_files: List[Path] = []
    per_file_json_path: Optional[Path] = None
    poler_md_path: Optional[Path] = None
    if out_dir is not None:
        out_dir = Path(out_dir)
        out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

        # 12a. per_file_report.json — full per-file metrics.
        per_file_json_path = out_dir / "per_file_report.json"
        per_file_json_path.write_text(
            json.dumps(
                {
                    "directory": str(dir_path),
                    "extensions": exts,
                    "recursive": bool(recursive),
                    "keyword": chosen_keyword,
                    "aggregates": aggregates,
                    "per_file": per_file,
                    "outliers": outliers,
                },
                ensure_ascii=False, indent=2, default=str,
            ),
            encoding="utf-8",
        )
        written_files.append(per_file_json_path)
        log.info(f"  wrote {per_file_json_path}")

        # 12b. poler_directory.md — POLER's format_directory_markdown.
        poler_md_path = out_dir / "poler_directory.md"
        poler_md_path.write_text(poler_md, encoding="utf-8")
        written_files.append(poler_md_path)
        log.info(f"  wrote {poler_md_path}")

        # 12c. PNG artifacts — only if there are readable files.
        # Each PNG is wrapped in its own try/except so a single bad
        # rendering (e.g., matplotlib font warning on a huge matrix)
        # doesn't kill the whole recipe. We still record the path in
        # ``artifacts`` if the file actually got written.
        if n_readable >= 1:
            file_labels = [_short_name(fp) for fp in readable_files]

            def _try_render(name: str, fname: str, fn, *args, **kwargs) -> None:
                """Render one PNG. Non-fatal on failure."""
                p = out_dir / fname
                try:
                    fn(*args, out_path=p, **kwargs)
                    if p.exists():
                        artifacts[name] = p
                        written_files.append(p)
                    else:
                        log.warning(f"  {name}: savefig returned but "
                                    f"file not on disk")
                except Exception as e:
                    log.warning(f"  {name}: rendering failed: {e}")

            _try_render(
                "file_similarity", "01_file_similarity.png",
                _file_similarity_heatmap,
                sim_matrix, file_labels,
                title=f"File similarity (TF-IDF cosine) — {dir_path.name}",
            )

            # Outlier indices into readable_files (the PCA / similarity
            # work off readable_files, not per_file).
            outlier_indices_read: List[int] = []
            for o in top_outliers:
                for i, fp in enumerate(readable_files):
                    if fp.name == o["name"]:
                        outlier_indices_read.append(i)
                        break
            pca_title = (
                f"PCA projection of files — {dir_path.name} "
                f"(PC1={pca_explained[0]:.1%}, PC2={pca_explained[1]:.1%})"
                if n_readable >= 2
                else f"PCA projection of files — {dir_path.name}"
            )
            _try_render(
                "pca_files", "02_pca_files.png",
                _pca_files_scatter,
                pca_coords, file_labels,
                explained_variance=pca_explained,
                outlier_idx=outlier_indices_read,
                title=pca_title,
            )

            _try_render(
                "wordcounts", "03_wordcounts.png",
                _wordcounts_bar,
                file_labels,
                [m["word_count"]
                 for m in per_file if m["read_error"] is None],
                title=f"Word count per file (sorted) — {dir_path.name}",
            )

            _try_render(
                "entropy_dist", "04_entropy_dist.png",
                _entropy_hist,
                [m["shannon_entropy"]
                 for m in per_file if m["read_error"] is None],
                title=f"Shannon entropy distribution — {dir_path.name}",
            )

            _try_render(
                "outliers_radar", "05_outliers_radar.png",
                _outliers_radar,
                outlier_radar_metrics,
                title=f"Outlier radar (top {len(top_outliers)}) — "
                      f"{dir_path.name}",
            )
        else:
            log.warning(
                "Skipping PNG artifacts: no readable files in directory",
            )

    # ----- 13. verdict -------------------------------------------------
    # Auto-verdict text.
    if n_files == 0:
        verdict_text = "ДИРЕКТОРИЯ ПУСТА — нет файлов для анализа."
    elif n_readable == 0:
        verdict_text = (
            f"НЕ УДАЛОСЬ ПРОЧИТАТЬ НИ ОДИН ФАЙЛ из {n_files} найденных. "
            "Проверьте кодировку / права доступа."
        )
    else:
        out_count = len(outliers)
        out_pct = (out_count / n_readable * 100) if n_readable else 0.0
        poler_hits = poler_summary.get("files_with_hits", 0)
        verdict_text = (
            f"Файлов: {n_files} (читаемых: {n_readable}). "
            f"Всего слов: {aggregates['total_words']:,}, "
            f"ср. длина: {aggregates['mean_word_count']:.0f} слов, "
            f"ср. энтропия: {aggregates['mean_entropy']:.2f} бит. "
            f"Аномалий (outliers): {out_count} ({out_pct:.0f}%) "
            f"при пороге cos<{OUTLIER_THRESHOLD}. "
            f"POLER «{chosen_keyword}»: совпадений в "
            f"{poler_hits}/{poler_summary.get('files_scanned', 0)} файлах, "
            f"всего фрагментов: {poler_summary.get('total_windows', 0)}."
        )

    # Aggregate metrics table.
    agg_rows = [
        ["Файлов найдено", str(n_files)],
        ["Читаемых", str(n_readable)],
        ["Расширения", ", ".join(exts)],
        ["Рекурсивно", "да" if recursive else "нет"],
        ["Keyword", chosen_keyword or "(пусто)"],
        ["Всего слов", f"{aggregates['total_words']:,}"],
        ["Всего символов", f"{aggregates['total_chars']:,}"],
        ["Всего строк", f"{aggregates['total_lines']:,}"],
        ["Ср. слов на файл", f"{aggregates['mean_word_count']:.1f}"],
        ["σ слов на файл", f"{aggregates['std_word_count']:.1f}"],
        ["Min слов", str(aggregates["min_word_count"])],
        ["Max слов", str(aggregates["max_word_count"])],
        ["Ср. энтропия", f"{aggregates['mean_entropy']:.3f}"],
        ["σ энтропии", f"{aggregates['std_entropy']:.3f}"],
        ["Ср. длина слова", f"{aggregates['mean_avg_word_length']:.2f}"],
        ["Outliers", f"{len(outliers)} (порог cos<{OUTLIER_THRESHOLD})"],
        ["POLER files_scanned",
         str(poler_summary.get("files_scanned", "—"))],
        ["POLER files_with_hits",
         str(poler_summary.get("files_with_hits", "—"))],
        ["POLER total_windows",
         str(poler_summary.get("total_windows", "—"))],
        ["POLER cross_clusters",
         str(poler_summary.get("cross_clusters_count", "—"))],
    ]
    if poler_summary.get("_error"):
        agg_rows.append(["POLER _error", "FAILED (see report.json)"])

    # Outlier table — top 5 (or fewer).
    out_rows: List[List[str]] = []
    for o in outliers[:5]:
        out_rows.append([
            o["name"],
            f"{o['max_cosine_to_other']:.3f}",
            str(o["word_count"]),
            f"{o['shannon_entropy']:.2f}",
            f"{o['avg_word_length']:.2f}",
        ])

    # Per-file summary table — top 10 by word count.
    per_file_sorted = sorted(
        (m for m in per_file if m["read_error"] is None),
        key=lambda m: -m["word_count"],
    )[:10]
    pf_rows: List[List[str]] = []
    for m in per_file_sorted:
        pf_rows.append([
            m["name"][:24],
            str(m["word_count"]),
            str(m["char_count"]),
            str(m["line_count"]),
            f"{m['shannon_entropy']:.2f}",
            f"{m['avg_word_length']:.2f}",
            ", ".join(f"{w}×{c}" for w, c in m["top_words"][:3]),
        ])

    verdict_lines = report.verdict_block(
        f"Batch Directory: {dir_path.name}",
        report.metric_table(["Метрика", "Значение"], agg_rows),
        (
            [
                "## Outliers (топ-5 по изоляции)",
                "",
                "| Файл | max cos | Слов | Энтропия | Ср. длина слова |",
                "|------|---------|------|----------|-----------------|",
                *(
                    f"| {r[0]} | {r[1]} | {r[2]} | {r[3]} | {r[4]} |"
                    for r in out_rows
                ),
                "",
            ]
            if out_rows
            else ["## Outliers", "", f"Ни один файл не ниже порога "
                 f"cos<{OUTLIER_THRESHOLD} — аномалий нет.", ""]
        ),
        [
            "## Топ-10 файлов (по числу слов)",
            "",
            "| Файл | Слов | Символов | Строк | Энтропия | Ср. длина | Топ-3 слова |",
            "|------|------|----------|-------|----------|-----------|-------------|",
            *(
                f"| {r[0]} | {r[1]} | {r[2]} | {r[3]} | {r[4]} | {r[5]} | {r[6]} |"
                for r in pf_rows
            ),
            "",
        ],
        ["## Вердикт", "", f"**{verdict_text}**", ""],
    )

    # ----- 14. wrap into standard report bundle -----------------------
    full_report = report.wrap_report(
        task_name="batch_directory",
        engine="POLER v6 analyze_directory + TF-IDF cosine + PCA + outlier detection",
        input_files=[str(dir_path)],
        metrics=metrics,
        output_files={k: str(v) for k, v in artifacts.items()},
        verdict=verdict_text,
        extra={
            "per_file_report_path": str(per_file_json_path)
                if per_file_json_path else None,
            "poler_directory_md_path": str(poler_md_path)
                if poler_md_path else None,
            "n_files": n_files,
            "n_readable": n_readable,
            "n_outliers": len(outliers),
            "files_written": [str(p) for p in written_files],
        },
    )

    return {
        "report": full_report,
        "verdict_lines": verdict_lines,
        "artifacts": artifacts,
    }


__all__ = ["recipe_batch_directory"]


# ============================================================
# Standalone test entry-point
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# Run:  python3 poler_toolkit/recipes_ext/batch_directory.py [DIR]
# If no dir given, defaults to $POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_DIR or /tmp/test_batch/.
# Output: $POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/test_batch_<ts>/ with 5 PNG + 4 text artifacts.
# ============================================================
if __name__ == "__main__":
    import sys
    import os
    from datetime import datetime

    logging.basicConfig(
        level=logging.INFO,
        format="%(asctime)s [%(name)s] %(levelname)s: %(message)s",
    )

    if len(sys.argv) >= 2:
        test_dir = sys.argv[1]
    else:
        test_dir = os.environ.get("POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_DIR", "/tmp/test_batch/")
        if not os.path.isdir(test_dir):
            print(f"Default test directory not found: {test_dir}")
            print("Set $POLER_TOOLKIT_TEST_DIR or pass as argument.")
            sys.exit(1)

    # Output dir via paths.py (env-aware)
    from .. import paths as _paths
    ts = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
    out_dir = _paths.get_output_root() / f"test_batch_{ts}"
    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    print(f"Running batch_directory on: {test_dir}")
    print(f"Output dir: {out_dir}")

    bundle = recipe_batch_directory(
        [test_dir],
        keyword=None,
        extensions=None,
        recursive=True,
        top_n=10,
        out_dir=out_dir,
    )

    # Write report.json + verdict.md (mirrors what runner.run() does).
    (out_dir / "report.json").write_text(
        json.dumps(
            bundle["report"], ensure_ascii=False, indent=2, default=str,
        ),
        encoding="utf-8",
    )
    (out_dir / "verdict.md").write_text(
        "\n".join(bundle["verdict_lines"]), encoding="utf-8",
    )

    print("\n=== DONE ===")
    print(f"Artifacts ({len(bundle['artifacts'])}):")
    for name, p in bundle["artifacts"].items():
        print(f"  {name:20s} -> {p}  (exists={p.exists()})")
    print(f"\nper_file_report.json: {out_dir / 'per_file_report.json'}")
    print(f"poler_directory.md:   {out_dir / 'poler_directory.md'}")
    print(f"report.json:          {out_dir / 'report.json'}")
    print(f"verdict.md:           {out_dir / 'verdict.md'}")
    print(f"\nVerdict: {bundle['report']['verdict']}")

    # Print outlier summary.
    cf = bundle["report"]["metrics"]["cross_file"]
    print(f"\nOutliers ({len(cf['outliers'])}):")
    for o in cf["outliers"]:
        print(f"  {o['name']:30s}  max_cos={o['max_cosine_to_other']:.3f}  "
              f"words={o['word_count']}  H={o['shannon_entropy']:.2f}")
```
