```
```
"""
poler_toolkit.recipes_ext.custom_plugin
=======================================

Recipe: load and run an external Python plugin file, then wrap its output
in the standard poler_toolkit report format.

POLER[n] framing
----------------
* **℘ (Pattern)** — user wants to run their own Python code on a file:
  this recipe is the toolkit's plugin system.
* **O (Operational)** — user writes ``my_plugin.py`` with
  ``def run(filepaths, opts, poler, toolkit_core, toolkit_viz, out_dir)``
  and the toolkit loads, validates, runs, and wraps the result.
* **L (Logic)** — sandbox-lite: import user's module via
  ``importlib.util.spec_from_file_location``, install warning hooks for
  ``os.environ`` / ``subprocess`` / ``socket`` (allowed but logged),
  call ``run()`` inside a ``ThreadPoolExecutor`` with a hard timeout,
  validate the return shape against the plugin contract, then wrap in
  the standard toolkit bundle.
* **ε (Energy)** — fail loudly, never silently. Every violation of the
  plugin contract raises ``RecipeError`` with a helpful hint. Every
  exception inside the plugin is captured and surfaced as
  ``recipe_error`` in the report (the recipe itself does not crash).
* **R[n] (Resonance)** — multi-level: plugin execution result + standard
  toolkit report (JSON + Markdown) + auto-generated ``plugin_info.md``
  (signature, docstring, module constants, execution time, peak memory).

What the recipe does
--------------------
1. Resolve ``plugin_path``: either from the explicit kwarg, or (if
   ``plugin_path is None``) from ``filepaths[0]`` when it ends in
   ``.py``. The remaining entries in ``filepaths`` become the target
   files passed to the plugin.
2. Load the plugin module via
   ``importlib.util.spec_from_file_location`` + ``spec.loader.exec_module``.
3. Validate that the module defines a callable ``run``. Raise
   ``RecipeError`` if not.
4. Install a *lite* sandbox:
   * ``os.environ`` access is wrapped in a logging proxy (allowed, but
     every read is logged at WARNING level).
   * ``subprocess.run / Popen / call / check_call / check_output`` are
     wrapped to log a WARNING before delegating to the real function
     (allowed).
   * ``socket.socket`` is wrapped to log a WARNING before delegating
     (allowed — we cannot prevent network access; we just log).
   All wrappers are restored in a ``finally`` block.
5. Start ``tracemalloc`` (for Python-allocated peak memory) and record
   ``resource.getrusage(RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss`` (for process RSS).
6. Run ``plugin.run(filepaths, opts, poler, toolkit_core, toolkit_viz,
   out_dir)`` inside a ``concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor`` with
   ``timeout=timeout`` seconds. If the future does not complete in
   time, mark the plugin as ``recipe_error`` with ``TimeoutError``;
   the worker thread is abandoned (daemonised) so it cannot block
   process exit.
7. Validate the return shape:
   * must be a ``dict``
   * must contain ``metrics`` (dict), ``verdict`` (str),
     ``artifacts`` (dict)
   * ``extra_lines`` (list of str) is optional
   Raise ``RecipeError`` with a helpful hint on any violation.
8. Generate ``plugin_info.md`` with:
   * plugin file path
   * function signature (via ``inspect.signature``)
   * function docstring (via ``inspect.getdoc``)
   * module-level constants (UPPER_CASE non-callable attributes)
   * execution time (wall clock)
   * peak memory (tracemalloc peak + RSS delta)
9. Wrap plugin metrics / verdict / artifacts in the standard
   poler_toolkit bundle via ``report.wrap_report`` +
   ``report.verdict_block`` + ``report.metric_table``.

Function signature
------------------
::

    def recipe_custom_plugin(
        filepaths: list,             # target files for the plugin
        *,
        plugin_path: Optional[str] = None,  # path to .py file with run()
        timeout: int = 300,
        plugin_opts: Optional[dict] = None,
        out_dir: Path = None,
    ) -> dict:
        # Returns {"report": dict, "verdict_lines": list, "artifacts": dict}

Plugin contract
---------------
The plugin file is a Python module that must define::

    def run(
        filepaths: list,           # list of Path objects
        opts: dict,                # user-supplied options
        poler,                     # poler_v6 module (already imported)
        toolkit_core,              # poler_toolkit.core module
        toolkit_viz,               # poler_toolkit.viz module
        out_dir,                   # Path to output directory
    ) -> dict:
        \"\"\"
        Must return a dict with at least:
            {
                "metrics": dict,        # arbitrary metrics
                "verdict": str,         # one-line verdict
                "artifacts": dict,      # {name: Path} — PNG files etc.
                "extra_lines": list,    # optional — additional Markdown lines
            }
        \"\"\"

RECIPES dict entry (for main agent)
-----------------------------------
::

    "custom_plugin": {
        "fn": recipe_custom_plugin,   # import from .recipes_ext.custom_plugin
        "n_files": None,              # SPECIAL: variable, needs CLI special-case
        "description": "Load and run an external Python plugin file; wraps "
                       "plugin output in standard toolkit report format. "
                       "Sandbox-lite: timeout + error capture + os.environ / "
                       "subprocess / socket warning hooks; auto-generated "
                       "plugin_info.md (signature, docstring, constants, "
                       "exec time, peak memory).",
        "options": ["plugin_path", "timeout", "plugin_opts"],
    }

CLI integration note
--------------------
This recipe takes a plugin_path (``.py`` file) PLUS a variable-length
list of target files. The CLI dispatcher will need a special case. Two
supported calling conventions:

1. **Keyword form (preferred for CLI)** — pass ``--plugin-path PATH``
   plus one or more positional target files. The recipe is invoked as
   ``recipe_custom_plugin([target1, target2, ...], plugin_path=PATH)``.

2. **Positional form** — pass the plugin file as the FIRST positional
   argument, followed by target files. The recipe is invoked as
   ``recipe_custom_plugin([plugin.py, target1, target2, ...])`` and
   internally detects that ``filepaths[0]`` ends in ``.py``.

A suggested CLI sketch::

    python -m poler_toolkit custom_plugin \\
        --plugin-path /path/to/my_plugin.py \\
        file1.txt file2.txt file3.txt \\
        [--timeout 300] [--opt key=value ...]

Or::

    python -m poler_toolkit custom_plugin \\
        /path/to/my_plugin.py file1.txt file2.txt \\
        [--timeout 300]

The CLI should parse ``--opt key=value`` into ``plugin_opts={key: value}``
(multiplicity allowed). ``--timeout`` is a plain int. ``n_files`` in
the RECIPES entry is ``None`` to signal "variable" — the runner.run()
file-count check must be skipped for this recipe (special case).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import importlib.util
import inspect
import json
import logging
import os
import resource
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import tracemalloc
import traceback
import concurrent.futures
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

# Recipe lives in poler_toolkit.recipes_ext, so relative imports match
# Stage 2 integration contract. Fallback: absolute imports, so the file
# can also be invoked directly as
# ``python3 .../custom_plugin.py PLUGIN.py TARGET...`` for testing.
try:
    from .. import core
    from .. import report
    from .. import viz
    from ..errors import (
        RecipeError, ConfigurationError, FileError,
    )
except ImportError:  # pragma: no cover - script-mode fallback
    import os as _os
    import sys as _sys
    _here = _os.path.dirname(_os.path.abspath(__file__))
    _scripts = _os.path.dirname(_os.path.dirname(_here))
    if _scripts not in _sys.path:
        _sys.path.insert(0, _scripts)
    from poler_toolkit import core  # type: ignore
    from poler_toolkit import report  # type: ignore
    from poler_toolkit import viz  # type: ignore
    from poler_toolkit.errors import (  # type: ignore
        RecipeError, ConfigurationError, FileError,
    )

log = logging.getLogger("poler_toolkit.recipes_ext.custom_plugin")


# ============================================================
# Tunable constants
# ============================================================
DEFAULT_TIMEOUT = 300
"""Default per-plugin execution timeout in seconds."""

REQUIRED_RETURN_KEYS = ("metrics", "verdict", "artifacts")
"""Keys that the plugin's ``run()`` return dict must contain."""

OPTIONAL_RETURN_KEYS = ("extra_lines",)
"""Optional keys in the plugin's return dict."""

_PLUGIN_MODULE_PREFIX = "poler_toolkit_custom_plugin_"
"""Module-name prefix used when loading the user's plugin. A unique
suffix (the file stem) is appended so reloading the same plugin twice
doesn't collide in ``sys.modules``."""

_MAX_MODULE_CONSTANTS = 50
"""Cap on the number of module-level constants we surface in
``plugin_info.md``. Guards against pathological plugins that have
hundreds of UPPER_CASE attributes."""

_MAX_CONST_REPR_LEN = 200
"""Truncate repr() of module constants to this many characters in
``plugin_info.md`` so we don't dump a 10 MB string into the doc."""


# ============================================================
# Plugin loading + validation
# ============================================================
def _resolve_plugin_path(
    filepaths: List[Any],
    plugin_path: Optional[str],
) -> Tuple[Path, List[Path]]:
    """Resolve which entry is the plugin path and which are targets.

    Supports two conventions (see module docstring):

    1. ``plugin_path`` kwarg given — ``filepaths`` is the target list.
    2. ``plugin_path`` is None — peek at ``filepaths[0]``: if it ends
       with ``.py``, treat it as the plugin; the rest are targets.

    Returns ``(plugin_path, target_paths)`` as ``Path`` objects.
    Raises ``ConfigurationError`` if no plugin path can be determined.
    """
    if plugin_path is not None:
        pp = Path(plugin_path)
        targets = [Path(fp) for fp in filepaths]
        return pp, targets

    # No explicit plugin_path — peek at filepaths[0].
    if not filepaths:
        raise ConfigurationError(
            "custom_plugin requires a plugin_path (a .py file with a "
            "run() function).",
            hint="Pass plugin_path='/path/to/my_plugin.py' OR put the "
                 "plugin .py file as the first entry of filepaths.",
        )
    first = Path(filepaths[0])
    if first.suffix.lower() == ".py":
        pp = first
        targets = [Path(fp) for fp in filepaths[1:]]
        return pp, targets

    raise ConfigurationError(
        f"custom_plugin: no plugin_path given and filepaths[0] "
        f"({first}) is not a .py file.",
        hint="Pass plugin_path='/path/to/my_plugin.py' OR make sure "
             "the first entry of filepaths is a .py file.",
    )


def _load_plugin_module(plugin_path: Path):
    """Load the plugin file as a Python module via importlib.

    Raises ``RecipeError`` if the file doesn't exist or can't be loaded.
    """
    if not plugin_path.exists():
        raise FileError(
            str(plugin_path),
            "plugin file not found",
            hint="Check the path you passed to --plugin-path.",
        )
    if not plugin_path.is_file():
        raise FileError(
            str(plugin_path),
            "plugin path is not a regular file",
            hint="plugin_path must point to a .py file.",
        )
    if plugin_path.suffix.lower() != ".py":
        raise RecipeError(
            f"plugin file must have .py extension, got '{plugin_path.suffix}'",
            hint=f"Rename the file to '{plugin_path.stem}.py' or pass "
                 f"a different plugin_path.",
        )

    mod_name = f"{_PLUGIN_MODULE_PREFIX}{plugin_path.stem}_{id(plugin_path)}"
    try:
        spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(mod_name, str(plugin_path))
        if spec is None or spec.loader is None:
            raise ImportError(
                f"spec_from_file_location returned None for {plugin_path}"
            )
        module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
        # Register in sys.modules so plugin can do relative imports of
        # its own submodules if needed (rare, but harmless).
        sys.modules[mod_name] = module
        spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    except RecipeError:
        raise
    except Exception as e:
        tb = traceback.format_exc()
        raise RecipeError(
            f"failed to import plugin module '{plugin_path}': "
            f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}",
            hint=f"Fix the import-time error in {plugin_path}:\n{tb}",
        ) from e
    return module


def _validate_plugin_callable(module, plugin_path: Path):
    """Verify that the plugin module has a callable ``run`` attribute.

    Raises ``RecipeError`` with a helpful hint if not.
    """
    run_fn = getattr(module, "run", None)
    if run_fn is None:
        raise RecipeError(
            f"plugin '{plugin_path}' does not define a `run` function",
            hint="Add `def run(filepaths, opts, poler, toolkit_core, "
                 "toolkit_viz, out_dir) -> dict:` to your plugin file.",
        )
    if not callable(run_fn):
        raise RecipeError(
            f"plugin '{plugin_path}' has a `run` attribute but it is not "
            f"callable (type={type(run_fn).__name__})",
            hint="Make sure `run` is a function, not a variable.",
        )
    return run_fn


def _validate_plugin_return(result: Any, plugin_path: Path) -> dict:
    """Validate the dict returned by the plugin's ``run()``.

    Raises ``RecipeError`` with a helpful hint on any contract violation.
    """
    if not isinstance(result, dict):
        raise RecipeError(
            f"plugin '{plugin_path}' run() returned {type(result).__name__}, "
            f"expected dict",
            hint="The plugin's run() must return a dict with keys: "
                 "'metrics' (dict), 'verdict' (str), 'artifacts' (dict), "
                 "and optionally 'extra_lines' (list of str).",
        )
    missing = [k for k in REQUIRED_RETURN_KEYS if k not in result]
    if missing:
        raise RecipeError(
            f"plugin '{plugin_path}' run() return dict is missing required "
            f"key(s): {missing}",
            hint=f"Add the missing key(s) {missing} to the dict returned "
                 f"by run(). Required keys: {list(REQUIRED_RETURN_KEYS)}.",
        )
    if not isinstance(result["metrics"], dict):
        raise RecipeError(
            f"plugin '{plugin_path}' return: 'metrics' must be a dict, "
            f"got {type(result['metrics']).__name__}",
            hint="The 'metrics' value should be a JSON-serialisable dict "
                 "of arbitrary metrics.",
        )
    if not isinstance(result["verdict"], str):
        raise RecipeError(
            f"plugin '{plugin_path}' return: 'verdict' must be a str, "
            f"got {type(result['verdict']).__name__}",
            hint="The 'verdict' value should be a one-line summary string.",
        )
    if not isinstance(result["artifacts"], dict):
        raise RecipeError(
            f"plugin '{plugin_path}' return: 'artifacts' must be a dict, "
            f"got {type(result['artifacts']).__name__}",
            hint="The 'artifacts' value should be a dict of {name: Path}.",
        )
    if "extra_lines" in result:
        el = result["extra_lines"]
        if not isinstance(el, list) or not all(isinstance(x, str) for x in el):
            raise RecipeError(
                f"plugin '{plugin_path}' return: 'extra_lines' must be a "
                f"list of str, got {type(el).__name__}",
                hint="Make extra_lines a list of strings (Markdown lines) "
                     "or omit the key entirely.",
            )
    return result


# ============================================================
# Sandbox-lite: logging wrappers for os.environ / subprocess / socket
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# These wrappers do NOT block access — they only log a WARNING when the
# plugin touches the corresponding resource. The original behaviour is
# fully preserved (return values, side effects, exceptions). Everything
# is restored in a finally block by the SandboxMonitor context manager.
# ============================================================
class SandboxMonitor:
    """Context manager that installs logging wrappers around
    ``os.environ``, ``subprocess.*``, and ``socket.socket`` for the
    duration of the plugin's ``run()`` call.

    All wrappers are *transparent* — they delegate to the real
    implementation after logging — so the plugin's behaviour is
    unchanged. The warnings are recorded in ``self.events`` so the
    recipe can surface them in the report.
    """

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.events: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
        # Placeholders for the originals (set in __enter__).
        self._orig_environ: Optional[Any] = None
        self._orig_subprocess: Dict[str, Any] = {}
        self._orig_socket_socket: Optional[Any] = None

    def _log(self, category: str, detail: str) -> None:
        evt = {
            "category": category,
            "detail": detail,
            "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
        }
        self.events.append(evt)
        log.warning(f"[sandbox] plugin accessed {category}: {detail}")

    # ---- os.environ ----
    def _make_environ_proxy(self, real_environ) -> "Any":
        """Return a dict-like proxy that logs every read but delegates
        to the real os.environ."""
        monitor = self

        class _LoggingEnviron:
            """Read-through proxy for os.environ.

            Reads log a warning and delegate to the real environ.
            Writes (``__setitem__``, ``__delitem__``, ``setdefault``,
            ``pop``, ``update``) propagate to the real environ so the
            plugin's mutations persist after the sandbox is torn down
            (matching normal Python semantics).
            """

            def __getitem__(self, key):
                monitor._log("os.environ", f"__getitem__({key!r})")
                return real_environ[key]

            def __contains__(self, key):
                monitor._log("os.environ", f"__contains__({key!r})")
                return key in real_environ

            def get(self, key, default=None):
                monitor._log("os.environ", f"get({key!r})")
                return real_environ.get(key, default)

            def __iter__(self):
                monitor._log("os.environ", "__iter__")
                return iter(real_environ)

            def __len__(self):
                monitor._log("os.environ", "__len__")
                return len(real_environ)

            def keys(self):
                monitor._log("os.environ", "keys")
                return real_environ.keys()

            def values(self):
                monitor._log("os.environ", "values")
                return real_environ.values()

            def items(self):
                monitor._log("os.environ", "items")
                return real_environ.items()

            def __setitem__(self, key, value):
                monitor._log("os.environ", f"__setitem__({key!r})")
                real_environ[key] = value

            def __delitem__(self, key):
                monitor._log("os.environ", f"__delitem__({key!r})")
                del real_environ[key]

            def setdefault(self, key, default=None):
                monitor._log("os.environ", f"setdefault({key!r})")
                return real_environ.setdefault(key, default)

            def pop(self, key, *args):
                monitor._log("os.environ", f"pop({key!r})")
                return real_environ.pop(key, *args)

            def update(self, *args, **kwargs):
                monitor._log("os.environ", "update")
                real_environ.update(*args, **kwargs)

            def copy(self):
                monitor._log("os.environ", "copy")
                return real_environ.copy()

            # Anything else → delegate to the real environ (no log,
            # because we can't enumerate every method and the plugin
            # might rely on weird internal APIs).
            def __getattr__(self, name):
                return getattr(real_environ, name)

        return _LoggingEnviron()

    # ---- subprocess ----
    def _wrap_subprocess(self) -> None:
        """Wrap subprocess.run / Popen / call / check_call / check_output."""
        monitor = self

        def _make_wrapper(name: str, original):
            def _wrapper(*args, **kwargs):
                # Truncate the args repr so we don't dump a 10 MB shell
                # command into the log.
                arg_preview = ", ".join(repr(a)[:120] for a in args[:3])
                monitor._log("subprocess",
                             f"{name}({arg_preview}, ...)")
                return original(*args, **kwargs)
            _wrapper.__name__ = f"_sandbox_{name}"
            _wrapper.__doc__ = original.__doc__
            return _wrapper

        for fn_name in ("run", "Popen", "call", "check_call",
                        "check_output"):
            original = getattr(subprocess, fn_name, None)
            if original is None:
                continue
            self._orig_subprocess[fn_name] = original
            setattr(subprocess, fn_name, _make_wrapper(fn_name, original))

    def _restore_subprocess(self) -> None:
        for fn_name, original in self._orig_subprocess.items():
            setattr(subprocess, fn_name, original)
        self._orig_subprocess.clear()

    # ---- socket ----
    def _wrap_socket(self) -> None:
        monitor = self
        original = socket.socket

        def _socket_wrapper(*args, **kwargs):
            arg_preview = ", ".join(repr(a)[:80] for a in args[:3])
            monitor._log("socket", f"socket({arg_preview}, ...)")
            return original(*args, **kwargs)

        # Preserve introspection attributes.
        try:
            _socket_wrapper.__name__ = "_sandbox_socket"
            _socket_wrapper.__doc__ = original.__doc__
        except AttributeError:
            pass
        self._orig_socket_socket = original
        socket.socket = _socket_wrapper  # type: ignore[assignment]

    def _restore_socket(self) -> None:
        if self._orig_socket_socket is not None:
            socket.socket = self._orig_socket_socket  # type: ignore[assignment]
            self._orig_socket_socket = None

    # ---- context-manager protocol ----
    def __enter__(self) -> "SandboxMonitor":
        # os.environ: swap in our logging proxy.
        self._orig_environ = os.environ
        os.environ = self._make_environ_proxy(self._orig_environ)  # type: ignore[assignment]
        self._wrap_subprocess()
        self._wrap_socket()
        return self

    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:
        # Restore in reverse order. We deliberately swallow nothing —
        # the plugin's exception (if any) propagates to the caller.
        try:
            if self._orig_environ is not None:
                os.environ = self._orig_environ  # type: ignore[assignment]
                self._orig_environ = None
        except Exception as e:
            log.error(f"[sandbox] failed to restore os.environ: {e}")
        try:
            self._restore_subprocess()
        except Exception as e:
            log.error(f"[sandbox] failed to restore subprocess: {e}")
        try:
            self._restore_socket()
        except Exception as e:
            log.error(f"[sandbox] failed to restore socket: {e}")


# ============================================================
# Resource tracking
# ============================================================
class ResourceTracker:
    """Measure wall-clock time + Python-allocated peak memory + process
    RSS delta around a code block.

    Uses ``tracemalloc`` for Python-allocated memory (precise but
    doesn't see C-extension allocations) and ``resource.getrusage`` for
    process RSS (whole-process, in kilobytes on Linux).
    """

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.elapsed_seconds: float = 0.0
        self.tracemalloc_peak_kb: float = 0.0
        self.tracemalloc_current_kb: float = 0.0
        self.rss_before_kb: int = 0
        self.rss_after_kb: int = 0
        self.rss_delta_kb: int = 0
        self._started_tracemalloc: bool = False
        self._t0: float = 0.0

    def __enter__(self) -> "ResourceTracker":
        if not tracemalloc.is_tracing():
            tracemalloc.start()
            self._started_tracemalloc = True
        else:
            tracemalloc.reset_peak()
        self._t0 = time.perf_counter()
        try:
            self.rss_before_kb = int(
                resource.getrusage(resource.RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss
            )
        except Exception:
            self.rss_before_kb = 0
        return self

    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:
        self.elapsed_seconds = time.perf_counter() - self._t0
        try:
            current, peak = tracemalloc.get_traced_memory()
            self.tracemalloc_current_kb = current / 1024.0
            self.tracemalloc_peak_kb = peak / 1024.0
        except Exception:
            pass
        if self._started_tracemalloc:
            try:
                tracemalloc.stop()
            except Exception:
                pass
        try:
            self.rss_after_kb = int(
                resource.getrusage(resource.RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss
            )
            self.rss_delta_kb = max(
                0, self.rss_after_kb - self.rss_before_kb
            )
        except Exception:
            self.rss_after_kb = 0
            self.rss_delta_kb = 0


# ============================================================
# Auto-documentation: plugin_info.md
# ============================================================
def _extract_module_constants(module) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
    """Return a sorted list of (name, repr) for module-level UPPER_CASE
    non-callable attributes.

    Skips dunder attributes and names starting with ``_``. Caps the
    number of constants at ``_MAX_MODULE_CONSTANTS`` and truncates each
    repr at ``_MAX_CONST_REPR_LEN`` characters.
    """
    out: List[Tuple[str, str]] = []
    for name in dir(module):
        if name.startswith("_"):
            continue
        if not name.isupper():
            # Allow UPPER_CASE_WITH_UNDERSCORES — str.isupper() returns
            # True only if there's at least one cased char and all are
            # upper. Names with digits like 'API_V2' still work because
            # digits are not cased.
            continue
        try:
            value = getattr(module, name)
        except Exception:
            continue
        if callable(value):
            continue
        if isinstance(value, type(module)):
            # Skip sub-module objects.
            continue
        try:
            r = repr(value)
        except Exception as e:
            r = f"<repr failed: {e}>"
        if len(r) > _MAX_CONST_REPR_LEN:
            r = r[:_MAX_CONST_REPR_LEN] + "…"
        out.append((name, r))
        if len(out) >= _MAX_MODULE_CONSTANTS:
            break
    out.sort(key=lambda kv: kv[0])
    return out


def _format_signature(run_fn) -> str:
    """Return a readable signature string for run_fn."""
    try:
        sig = inspect.signature(run_fn)
        return f"run{sig}"
    except (ValueError, TypeError) as e:
        return f"run(...)  # signature unavailable: {e}"


def _build_plugin_info_md(
    plugin_path: Path,
    module,
    run_fn,
    elapsed_seconds: float,
    rss_delta_kb: int,
    tracemalloc_peak_kb: float,
    sandbox_events: List[Dict[str, Any]],
    plugin_opts: Dict[str, Any],
    n_targets: int,
    error: Optional[Dict[str, Any]],
) -> List[str]:
    """Build the Markdown lines for ``plugin_info.md``."""
    docstring = inspect.getdoc(run_fn) or "(no docstring)"
    sig = _format_signature(run_fn)
    constants = _extract_module_constants(module)
    mod_doc = inspect.getdoc(module) or "(no module docstring)"

    lines: List[str] = []
    lines.append(f"# Plugin info: {plugin_path.name}")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append(f"Auto-generated by `recipe_custom_plugin` at "
                 f"{datetime.now().isoformat(timespec='seconds')}.")
    lines.append("")

    lines.append("## File")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append(f"- **Path**: `{plugin_path}`")
    try:
        size = plugin_path.stat().st_size
        lines.append(f"- **Size**: {size:,} bytes")
    except Exception:
        pass
    lines.append(f"- **Targets passed**: {n_targets}")
    lines.append("")

    lines.append("## Module docstring")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("```")
    lines.append(mod_doc)
    lines.append("```")
    lines.append("")

    lines.append("## Function signature")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("```python")
    lines.append(sig)
    lines.append("```")
    lines.append("")

    lines.append("## Function docstring")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("```")
    lines.append(docstring)
    lines.append("```")
    lines.append("")

    lines.append("## Module-level constants")
    lines.append("")
    if constants:
        lines.append("| Name | Value (repr) |")
        lines.append("|------|--------------|")
        for name, r in constants:
            # Escape pipes in the value so we don't break the table.
            r_safe = r.replace("|", "\\|")
            lines.append(f"| `{name}` | `{r_safe}` |")
        lines.append("")
    else:
        lines.append("(no UPPER_CASE module-level constants found)")
        lines.append("")

    lines.append("## Plugin options")
    lines.append("")
    if plugin_opts:
        lines.append("| Key | Value |")
        lines.append("|-----|-------|")
        for k, v in plugin_opts.items():
            try:
                r = repr(v)
            except Exception as e:
                r = f"<repr failed: {e}>"
            if len(r) > _MAX_CONST_REPR_LEN:
                r = r[:_MAX_CONST_REPR_LEN] + "…"
            r_safe = r.replace("|", "\\|")
            lines.append(f"| `{k}` | `{r_safe}` |")
        lines.append("")
    else:
        lines.append("_(no opts passed — empty dict)_")
        lines.append("")

    lines.append("## Execution")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append(f"- **Wall-clock time**: {elapsed_seconds:.3f} s")
    lines.append(f"- **tracemalloc peak**: "
                 f"{tracemalloc_peak_kb:,.1f} KiB "
                 f"(Python-allocated only)")
    lines.append(f"- **RSS delta (ru_maxrss)**: "
                 f"{rss_delta_kb:,} KiB")
    if error is not None:
        lines.append(f"- **Status**: FAILED ({error.get('type', '?')})")
    else:
        lines.append("- **Status**: OK")
    lines.append("")

    lines.append("## Sandbox events")
    lines.append("")
    if sandbox_events:
        lines.append("The plugin accessed the following sandboxed "
                     "resources during execution. Access was ALLOWED "
                     "(only logged).")
        lines.append("")
        lines.append("| # | Category | Detail | Timestamp |")
        lines.append("|---|----------|--------|-----------|")
        for i, evt in enumerate(sandbox_events, 1):
            detail = str(evt.get("detail", "")).replace("|", "\\|")
            cat = str(evt.get("category", "")).replace("|", "\\|")
            ts = str(evt.get("timestamp", "")).replace("|", "\\|")
            lines.append(f"| {i} | {cat} | `{detail}` | {ts} |")
        lines.append("")
    else:
        lines.append("No sandboxed resources (os.environ / subprocess / "
                     "socket) were accessed.")
        lines.append("")

    return lines


# ============================================================
# Main recipe
# ============================================================
def recipe_custom_plugin(
    filepaths: list,
    *,
    plugin_path: Optional[str] = None,
    timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT,
    plugin_opts: Optional[dict] = None,
    out_dir: Path = None,
) -> dict:
    """Load and run an external Python plugin file.

    See module docstring for the full description and the plugin
    contract.

    Returns
    -------
    dict
        ``{"report": dict, "verdict_lines": list, "artifacts": dict}`` —
        the standard poler_toolkit bundle. If the plugin raised an
        exception, the bundle still has the standard structure but the
        report contains a ``recipe_error`` key with the failure details.
    """
    # ----- 1. resolve inputs ------------------------------------------
    opts = dict(plugin_opts) if plugin_opts else {}
    pp, targets = _resolve_plugin_path(filepaths, plugin_path)
    log.info(f"[custom_plugin] plugin={pp}  targets={len(targets)}  "
             f"timeout={timeout}s  opts_keys={list(opts.keys())}")

    if timeout <= 0:
        raise ConfigurationError(
            f"timeout must be > 0, got {timeout}",
            hint="Pass --timeout N with N >= 1.",
        )

    # Make sure out_dir exists (plugin may write files there).
    if out_dir is not None:
        out_dir = Path(out_dir)
        out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    else:
        # Even if out_dir is None we still pass a Path to the plugin —
        # use a tempdir-style fallback under the system temp. The
        # plugin contract requires out_dir to be a real Path the plugin
        # can write to.
        import tempfile
        out_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="poler_plugin_"))
        log.warning(f"[custom_plugin] out_dir was None — using tempdir "
                    f"{out_dir}")

    # ----- 2. load plugin module --------------------------------------
    module = _load_plugin_module(pp)
    run_fn = _validate_plugin_callable(module, pp)
    log.info(f"  loaded plugin module; run() signature: "
             f"{_format_signature(run_fn)}")

    # ----- 3. prepare toolkit references ------------------------------
    poler = core.get_poler()
    toolkit_core = core
    toolkit_viz = viz

    # Pass targets as Path objects (per plugin contract).
    target_paths: List[Path] = [Path(t) for t in targets]

    # ----- 4. run plugin in sandbox + thread + resource tracker -------
    sandbox = SandboxMonitor()
    tracker = ResourceTracker()
    plugin_error: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
    plugin_result: Optional[dict] = None
    timed_out: bool = False

    log.info(f"  running plugin.run() with timeout={timeout}s...")
    with sandbox, tracker:
        # NOTE: we deliberately do NOT use a `with` block on the executor
        # because its __exit__ calls shutdown(wait=True), which would
        # block until the worker thread finishes — defeating the timeout.
        # Instead we manage shutdown manually so that on timeout we can
        # abandon the worker (it's a daemon thread since Py 3.9, so it
        # won't block process exit either).
        ex = concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(
            max_workers=1, thread_name_prefix="custom_plugin_worker",
        )
        future = ex.submit(
            run_fn,
            target_paths,
            opts,
            poler,
            toolkit_core,
            toolkit_viz,
            out_dir,
        )
        try:
            plugin_result = future.result(timeout=timeout)
        except concurrent.futures.TimeoutError:
            timed_out = True
            plugin_error = {
                "type": "TimeoutError",
                "message": (f"plugin.run() did not complete within "
                            f"{timeout}s"),
                "traceback": None,
            }
            log.error(f"  TIMEOUT: plugin.run() exceeded {timeout}s")
            # Best-effort cancel + non-blocking shutdown. The worker
            # thread keeps running in the background (we cannot kill it
            # safely in CPython), but it's a daemon thread so it won't
            # block process exit.
            try:
                future.cancel()
            except Exception:
                pass
            try:
                # cancel_futures=True was added in Py 3.9 — try with it,
                # fall back to plain shutdown(wait=False) on older Pythons.
                try:
                    ex.shutdown(wait=False, cancel_futures=True)
                except TypeError:
                    ex.shutdown(wait=False)
            except Exception as e:
                log.warning(f"  executor shutdown after timeout failed: {e}")
        except Exception as e:
            tb_str = traceback.format_exc()
            plugin_error = {
                "type": type(e).__name__,
                "message": str(e),
                "traceback": tb_str,
            }
            log.error(f"  plugin.run() raised {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            log.debug(tb_str)
            # Worker already finished (it raised). shutdown(wait=False)
            # is non-blocking but still reaps the dead thread.
            try:
                ex.shutdown(wait=False)
            except Exception:
                pass
        else:
            # Normal completion — worker is done, shutdown is fast.
            try:
                ex.shutdown(wait=False)
            except Exception:
                pass

    log.info(f"  done in {tracker.elapsed_seconds:.3f}s "
             f"(tracemalloc peak={tracker.tracemalloc_peak_kb:.1f} KiB, "
             f"RSS delta={tracker.rss_delta_kb} KiB)")
    log.info(f"  sandbox events: {len(sandbox.events)}")

    # ----- 5. validate return shape (only if no error) ----------------
    if plugin_error is None and plugin_result is not None:
        try:
            plugin_result = _validate_plugin_return(plugin_result, pp)
        except RecipeError as e:
            # Contract violation — surface as recipe_error too.
            plugin_error = {
                "type": "PluginContractError",
                "message": str(e),
                "traceback": None,
            }
            log.error(f"  contract violation: {e}")
            plugin_result = None

    # ----- 6. build metrics / verdict / artifacts ---------------------
    artifacts: Dict[str, Path] = {}
    plugin_artifacts: Dict[str, Any] = {}
    extra_lines: List[str] = []
    plugin_metrics: Dict[str, Any] = {}
    verdict_text: str

    if plugin_error is not None:
        # Failure path — produce a graceful bundle.
        verdict_text = (
            f"PLUGIN FAILED ({plugin_error['type']}): "
            f"{plugin_error['message']}"
        )
        plugin_metrics = {
            "plugin_path": str(pp),
            "n_targets": len(target_paths),
            "status": "failed",
            "error": plugin_error,
            "execution_time_seconds": tracker.elapsed_seconds,
            "tracemalloc_peak_kib": tracker.tracemalloc_peak_kb,
            "rss_delta_kib": tracker.rss_delta_kb,
            "sandbox_events": sandbox.events,
        }
    else:
        # Success path — adopt plugin's metrics / artifacts / verdict.
        assert plugin_result is not None
        plugin_metrics = dict(plugin_result.get("metrics", {}))
        plugin_artifacts = dict(plugin_result.get("artifacts", {}))
        extra_lines = list(plugin_result.get("extra_lines", []) or [])
        verdict_text = str(plugin_result.get("verdict", ""))

        # Coerce artifact values to Path objects (plugin may have
        # returned strings). Keep only files that actually exist on
        # disk so the report doesn't promise artifacts that aren't there.
        for name, val in plugin_artifacts.items():
            try:
                p = Path(val)
            except Exception as e:
                log.warning(f"  artifact '{name}' is not a valid path "
                            f"({val!r}): {e}")
                continue
            if p.exists():
                artifacts[name] = p
            else:
                log.warning(f"  artifact '{name}' path does not exist: {p}")

        # Wrap plugin-supplied metrics so we always know what the
        # toolkit added vs what the plugin returned.
        plugin_metrics = {
            "plugin_metrics": plugin_metrics,
            "plugin_path": str(pp),
            "n_targets": len(target_paths),
            "status": "ok",
            "execution_time_seconds": tracker.elapsed_seconds,
            "tracemalloc_peak_kib": tracker.tracemalloc_peak_kb,
            "rss_delta_kib": tracker.rss_delta_kb,
            "sandbox_events": sandbox.events,
            "sandbox_events_count": len(sandbox.events),
        }

    # ----- 7. write plugin_info.md ------------------------------------
    plugin_info_path: Optional[Path] = None
    if out_dir is not None:
        try:
            info_lines = _build_plugin_info_md(
                plugin_path=pp,
                module=module,
                run_fn=run_fn,
                elapsed_seconds=tracker.elapsed_seconds,
                rss_delta_kb=tracker.rss_delta_kb,
                tracemalloc_peak_kb=tracker.tracemalloc_peak_kb,
                sandbox_events=sandbox.events,
                plugin_opts=opts,
                n_targets=len(target_paths),
                error=plugin_error,
            )
            plugin_info_path = out_dir / "plugin_info.md"
            plugin_info_path.write_text(
                "\n".join(info_lines), encoding="utf-8",
            )
            log.info(f"  wrote {plugin_info_path}")
            # Also surface it as an artifact so the runner lists it.
            artifacts["plugin_info"] = plugin_info_path
        except Exception as e:
            log.error(f"  failed to write plugin_info.md: {e}")

    # ----- 8. build verdict_lines (Markdown) --------------------------
    # Status row at the top.
    status_str = "FAILED" if plugin_error else "OK"
    header_rows: List[List[str]] = [
        ["Plugin path", str(pp)],
        ["Targets", str(len(target_paths))],
        ["Timeout", f"{timeout}s"],
        ["Execution time", f"{tracker.elapsed_seconds:.3f}s"],
        ["tracemalloc peak", f"{tracker.tracemalloc_peak_kb:,.1f} KiB"],
        ["RSS delta", f"{tracker.rss_delta_kb:,} KiB"],
        ["Sandbox events", str(len(sandbox.events))],
        ["Status", status_str],
    ]
    if plugin_error:
        header_rows.append(["Error type", plugin_error["type"]])
        # Truncate long error messages for the table.
        msg = plugin_error.get("message", "")
        if len(msg) > 200:
            msg = msg[:200] + "…"
        header_rows.append(["Error message", msg])

    # Sandbox-events summary table.
    sandbox_lines: List[str] = []
    if sandbox.events:
        # Aggregate by category.
        cat_counts: Dict[str, int] = {}
        for evt in sandbox.events:
            cat = evt.get("category", "?")
            cat_counts[cat] = cat_counts.get(cat, 0) + 1
        sandbox_lines = [
            "## Sandbox access log (allowed, logged)",
            "",
            "| Category | Count |",
            "|----------|-------|",
        ]
        for cat in sorted(cat_counts):
            sandbox_lines.append(
                f"| {cat} | {cat_counts[cat]} |"
            )
        sandbox_lines.append("")
        # Show first 10 events as detail.
        sandbox_lines.extend([
            "### First 10 events",
            "",
            "| # | Category | Detail |",
            "|---|----------|--------|",
        ])
        for i, evt in enumerate(sandbox.events[:10], 1):
            detail = str(evt.get("detail", "")).replace("|", "\\|")
            cat = str(evt.get("category", "")).replace("|", "\\|")
            sandbox_lines.append(f"| {i} | {cat} | `{detail}` |")
        sandbox_lines.append("")
    else:
        sandbox_lines = [
            "## Sandbox access log",
            "",
            "Plugin did not access os.environ / subprocess / socket.",
            "",
        ]

    # Plugin-supplied extra lines (if any).
    extra_section: List[str] = []
    if extra_lines:
        extra_section = ["## Plugin extra output", ""] + extra_lines + [""]

    # Error detail (full traceback) — only on failure.
    error_section: List[str] = []
    if plugin_error and plugin_error.get("traceback"):
        error_section = [
            "## Plugin error (traceback)",
            "",
            "```",
            str(plugin_error["traceback"]),
            "```",
            "",
        ]

    verdict_lines = report.verdict_block(
        f"Custom plugin: {pp.name}",
        report.metric_table(["Поле", "Значение"], header_rows),
        sandbox_lines,
        extra_section,
        error_section,
        ["## Вердикт", "", f"**{verdict_text}**", ""],
    )

    # ----- 9. wrap into standard report bundle ------------------------
    full_report = report.wrap_report(
        task_name="custom_plugin",
        engine=f"custom plugin ({pp.name}) + poler_toolkit wrapper",
        input_files=[str(pp)] + [str(t) for t in target_paths],
        metrics=plugin_metrics,
        output_files={k: str(v) for k, v in artifacts.items()},
        verdict=verdict_text,
        extra={
            "plugin_path": str(pp),
            "plugin_info_path": str(plugin_info_path)
                if plugin_info_path else None,
            "timeout_seconds": timeout,
            "timed_out": timed_out,
            "plugin_opts": opts,
            "n_targets": len(target_paths),
            "target_files": [str(t) for t in target_paths],
            "sandbox_events_count": len(sandbox.events),
            "execution_time_seconds": tracker.elapsed_seconds,
            "tracemalloc_peak_kib": tracker.tracemalloc_peak_kb,
            "rss_delta_kib": tracker.rss_delta_kb,
            "recipe_error": plugin_error,
        },
    )

    return {
        "report": full_report,
        "verdict_lines": verdict_lines,
        "artifacts": artifacts,
    }


__all__ = ["recipe_custom_plugin"]


# ============================================================
# Standalone test entry-point
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# Run:  python3 poler_toolkit/recipes_ext/custom_plugin.py \
#           PLUGIN.py [TARGET1 TARGET2 ...] [--timeout N]
# Output: $POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT/test_custom_plugin_<ts>/ with
# report.json + verdict.md + plugin_info.md + any plugin artifacts.
# ============================================================
if __name__ == "__main__":  # pragma: no cover
    import argparse as _ap
    import sys as _sys

    logging.basicConfig(
        level=logging.INFO,
        format="%(asctime)s [%(name)s] %(levelname)s: %(message)s",
    )

    parser = _ap.ArgumentParser(
        prog="custom_plugin",
        description="Load and run an external Python plugin file.",
    )
    parser.add_argument("plugin", help="Path to the plugin .py file.")
    parser.add_argument("targets", nargs="*", help="Target file paths.")
    parser.add_argument("--timeout", type=int, default=DEFAULT_TIMEOUT,
                        help=f"Plugin execution timeout in seconds "
                             f"(default {DEFAULT_TIMEOUT}).")
    parser.add_argument("--opt", action="append", default=[],
                        help="Plugin option, as key=value (repeatable).")
    parser.add_argument("--out-dir", default=None,
                        help="Output directory (default: auto-timestamped "
                             "under $POLER_TOOLKIT_OUTPUT).")
    args = parser.parse_args()

    # Parse plugin opts from --opt key=value pairs.
    plugin_opts: Dict[str, Any] = {}
    for kv in args.opt:
        if "=" not in kv:
            log.warning(f"ignoring --opt '{kv}' (no '=')")
            continue
        k, v = kv.split("=", 1)
        # Try to parse as int / float / bool / json, else keep as str.
        try:
            v_parsed = json.loads(v)
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            v_parsed = v
        plugin_opts[k] = v_parsed

    ts = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
    if args.out_dir:
        out_dir = Path(args.out_dir)
    else:
        from .. import paths as _paths
        out_dir = _paths.get_output_root() / f"test_custom_plugin_{ts}"
    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    print(f"Plugin:    {args.plugin}")
    print(f"Targets:   {args.targets}")
    print(f"Out dir:   {out_dir}")
    print(f"Timeout:   {args.timeout}s")
    print(f"Opts:      {plugin_opts}")
    print()

    # Call the recipe. Use the positional convention: filepaths[0] is
    # the plugin, filepaths[1:] are the targets. (The kwarg form
    # plugin_path=... is also supported.)
    bundle = recipe_custom_plugin(
        [args.plugin] + list(args.targets),
        plugin_path=None,
        timeout=args.timeout,
        plugin_opts=plugin_opts,
        out_dir=out_dir,
    )

    # Write report.json + verdict.md (mirrors what runner.run() does).
    (out_dir / "report.json").write_text(
        json.dumps(
            bundle["report"], ensure_ascii=False, indent=2, default=str,
        ),
        encoding="utf-8",
    )
    (out_dir / "verdict.md").write_text(
        "\n".join(bundle["verdict_lines"]), encoding="utf-8",
    )

    print("\n=== DONE ===")
    print(f"Artifacts ({len(bundle['artifacts'])}):")
    for name, p in bundle["artifacts"].items():
        print(f"  {name:20s} -> {p}  (exists={Path(p).exists()})")
    print(f"\nreport.json:   {out_dir / 'report.json'}")
    print(f"verdict.md:    {out_dir / 'verdict.md'}")
    print(f"plugin_info.md: {out_dir / 'plugin_info.md'}")
    print(f"\nVerdict: {bundle['report']['verdict']}")
    if bundle["report"].get("extra", {}).get("recipe_error"):
        err = bundle["report"]["extra"]["recipe_error"]
        print(f"\n[FAILED] {err.get('type')}: {err.get('message')}")
        _sys.exit(1)
